# LAW

LAW

of 20 May 2005

CRIMINAL CODE

The National Council of the Slovak Republic has adopted this law:

FIRST PART

GENERAL PART

## § 1

Subject of the Law

This law regulates the fundamentals of criminal liability, types of punishments, types of protective measures, their imposition and the elements of criminal offences.

FIRST TITLE

SCOPE OF THE LAW AND FUNDAMENTALS OF CRIMINAL LIABILITY

First Division

The Criminal Code and its Scope

## § 2

Temporal Scope

(1) The criminality of an act shall be assessed and punishment shall be imposed according to the law in force at the time when the act was committed. If several laws come into force between the time of commission of the act and the delivery of the judgment, the criminality of the act shall be assessed and punishment shall be imposed according to the law that is more favourable to the offender.

(2) Such type of punishment may be imposed on the offender as is permitted by the law in force at the time when the criminal offence is being decided, if this is more favourable to the offender.

(3) Unless this law provides otherwise, a protective measure shall be imposed according to the law in force at the time when the protective measure is being decided.

## § 3

Territorial Scope

(1) The criminality of an act committed on the territory of the Slovak Republic shall be assessed according to this law.

(2) A criminal offence shall be deemed to have been committed on the territory of the Slovak Republic even if the offender

a) performed the conduct at least partially on its territory, if the violation or endangerment of an interest protected by this law occurred or was to occur wholly or partially outside its territory, or

b) performed the conduct outside the territory of the Slovak Republic, if the violation or endangerment of an interest protected by this law was to occur there or if such a consequence was to occur there at least partially.

(3) The criminality of an act committed outside the territory of the Slovak Republic on board a vessel sailing under the state flag of the Slovak Republic or on board an aircraft registered in the aircraft register of the Slovak Republic shall also be assessed according to this law.

Personal Scope

## § 4

The criminality of an act committed outside the territory of the Slovak Republic by a citizen of the Slovak Republic or a foreign national who has permanent residence on the territory of the Slovak Republic shall also be assessed according to this law.

## § 5

The criminality of a particularly serious felony shall also be assessed according to this law if the act was committed outside the territory of the Slovak Republic against a citizen of the Slovak Republic and the act is punishable at the place of commission or if the place of commission is not subject to any criminal jurisdiction.

## § 5a

The criminality of unauthorised production of narcotic drugs and psychotropic substances, poisons or precursors, their possession and trafficking in them (§ 171 and 172), legalisation of proceeds from criminal activity (§ 233 to 234), counterfeiting, alteration and unauthorised production of money and securities (§ 270), uttering of counterfeit, altered and unauthorisedly produced money and securities (§ 271), production and possession of counterfeiting instruments (§ 272), counterfeiting, alteration and unauthorised production of revenue stamps, postal vouchers, stickers and postal stamps (§ 274), counterfeiting and alteration of control technical measures for marking goods (§ 275), establishment, conspiracy and support of a terrorist group or its member (§ 297), unauthorised production and possession of nuclear materials, radioactive substances, high-risk chemical substances and high-risk biological agents and toxins (§ 298 and 299), conspiracy against the Slovak Republic (§ 312), terror (§ 313 and 314), sabotage (§ 315 and 316), sabotage (§ 317), espionage (§ 318), attack on a public authority (§ 321), attack on a public official (§ 323), counterfeiting and alteration of public documents, official seal, official closure, official emblem and official mark (§ 352), endangerment of confidential facts and classified facts (§ 353), people smuggling (§ 355), endangerment of peace (§ 417), genocide (§ 418), terrorist attack (§ 419), certain forms of participation in terrorism (§ 419b), financing of terrorism (§ 419c), travelling for the purpose of terrorism (§ 419d), inhumanity (§ 425), use of prohibited means of combat and unlawful conduct of combat (§ 426), pillage in the area of military operations (§ 427), abuse of internationally recognised designations and state symbols (§ 428), war cruelty (§ 431), persecution of the population (§ 432), war lawlessness (§ 433) shall also be assessed according to this law if such a criminal offence was committed outside the territory of the Slovak Republic by a foreign national who does not have permanent residence on the territory of the Slovak Republic.

## § 6

(1) The criminality of an act committed outside the territory of the Slovak Republic by a foreign national who does not have permanent residence on the territory of the Slovak Republic shall also be assessed according to this law if

a) the act is also punishable under the law in force in the territory where it was committed,

b) the offender was detained or arrested on the territory of the Slovak Republic, and

c) he was not extradited for criminal prosecution to a foreign state.

(2) However, the offender referred to in subsection 1 may not be imposed a punishment more severe than that prescribed by the law of the state in whose territory the criminal offence was committed.

## § 7

Scope according to International Treaties

(1) The criminality of an act shall be assessed according to this law also when so provided by an international treaty that has been ratified and promulgated in the manner prescribed by law, by which the Slovak Republic is bound.

(2) The provisions of § 3 to 6 shall not be applied if not permitted by an international treaty that has been ratified and promulgated in the manner prescribed by law, by which the Slovak Republic is bound.

## § 7a

Scope for Imposition of Protective Measures

(1) A protective measure under this law may be imposed if the criminality of the act in connection with the commission of which the protective measure is to be imposed is assessed according to this law.

(2) The provision of subsection 1 shall also apply if the offender of an otherwise punishable act is not criminally liable or if it concerns a person who cannot be prosecuted or convicted.

## § 7b

Execution and Consideration of a Decision of Another State

(1) A decision of a court of another state in a criminal matter may be executed on the territory of the Slovak Republic or may have other legal effects only if so provided by an international treaty or law.

(2) A final conviction by a court of another Member State of the European Union in criminal proceedings shall be taken into account for the purposes of criminal proceedings in the same way as if it had been issued by a court of the Slovak Republic, if it was issued for an act that is also punishable under the legal order of the Slovak Republic.

Second Division

Fundamentals of Criminal Liability

FIRST SECTION

CONCEPT AND TYPES OF CRIMINAL OFFENCE

## § 8

Criminal Offence

A criminal offence is an unlawful act whose elements are set out in this law, unless this law provides otherwise.

## § 9

Types of Criminal Offences

A criminal offence is a misdemeanour and a felony.

## § 10

Misdemeanour

(1) A misdemeanour is

a) a criminal offence committed by negligence, or

b) an intentional criminal offence for which this law in the special part prescribes a punishment of imprisonment with an upper limit of the sentencing range not exceeding five years.

(2) It is not a misdemeanour if, having regard to the manner of execution of the act and its consequences, the circumstances under which the act was committed, the degree of culpability and the motive of the offender, its seriousness is negligible.

## § 11

Felony

(1) A felony is an intentional criminal offence for which this law in the special part prescribes a punishment of imprisonment with an upper limit of the sentencing range exceeding five years.

(2) It is also a felony if in a more severe element of a misdemeanour committed intentionally an upper limit of the sentencing range exceeding five years is prescribed.

(3) A felony for which this law prescribes a punishment of imprisonment with a lower limit of the sentencing range of at least ten years shall be considered particularly serious.

## § 12

Place of Commission of a Criminal Offence

The place of commission of a criminal offence is every place where

a) the offender acted, or

b) the consequence anticipated by this law occurred or, according to the perception of the offender, was to occur.

## § 13

Preparation for a Felony

(1) Preparation for a felony is conduct consisting of intentional organizing of a felony, procuring or adapting means or instruments for its commission, conspiring, assembling, instigating, ordering or aiding such a felony, or other intentional creation of conditions for its commission, if there was no attempt or completion of the felony.

(2) Preparation for a felony is punishable according to the sentence established for the felony towards which it was directed.

(3) Criminal liability for preparation for a felony ceases if the perpetrator voluntarily

a) desisted from further conduct directed towards the commission of the felony and eliminated the danger that arose to an interest protected by this Act from the undertaken preparation, or

b) made a report on the preparation for the felony to an authority active in criminal proceedings or to the Police Force at a time when the danger that arose to an interest protected by this Act from the undertaken preparation could still be eliminated; a soldier may make this report also to his superior or service authority and a person serving a sentence of imprisonment or in custody also to a member of the Prison and Judicial Guard Corps.

(4) The provision of paragraph 3 does not affect the criminal liability of the perpetrator for another criminal offence that he already committed by such conduct.

## § 14

Attempt of a Criminal Offence

(1) An attempt of a criminal offence is conduct that directly aims at the completion of a criminal offence, which the perpetrator committed with the intention to commit a criminal offence, if the criminal offence was not completed.

(2) An attempt of a criminal offence is punishable according to the sentence established for the completed criminal offence.

(3) Criminal liability for an attempt of a criminal offence ceases if the perpetrator voluntarily

a) desisted from further conduct necessary for the completion of the criminal offence and eliminated the danger that arose to an interest protected by this Act from the undertaken attempt, or

b) made a report on the attempt of a criminal offence to an authority active in criminal proceedings or to the Police Force at a time when the danger that arose to an interest protected by this Act from the undertaken attempt could still be eliminated; a soldier may make this report also to his superior or service authority and a person serving a sentence of imprisonment or in custody also to a member of the Prison and Judicial Guard Corps.

(4) The provision of paragraph 3 does not affect the criminal liability of the perpetrator for another criminal offence that he already committed by such conduct.

Culpability

## § 15

A criminal offence is committed intentionally if the perpetrator

a) wanted to violate or endanger an interest protected by this Act in the manner specified in this Act, or

b) knew that by his conduct he could cause such violation or endangerment, and in the event that he caused it, was reconciled with it.

## § 16

A criminal offence is committed negligently if the perpetrator

a) knew that he could violate or endanger an interest protected by this Act in the manner specified in this Act, but without reasonable grounds relied that he would not cause such violation or endangerment, or

b) did not know that by his conduct he could cause such violation or endangerment, although given the circumstances and his personal conditions he should and could have known about it.

## § 17

For the criminal liability of an act committed by a natural person, intentional culpability is required, unless this Act expressly provides that negligent culpability is sufficient.

## § 18

An aggravating circumstance or a circumstance that conditions the use of a higher sentence shall be taken into account if it concerns

a) a more serious consequence, even when the perpetrator caused it negligently, unless this Act requires intentional culpability also in this case, or

b) another fact, even when the perpetrator did not know about it, although given the circumstances and his personal conditions he should and could have known about it, unless this Act requires that the perpetrator knew about it.

SECOND PART

PERPETRATOR, CO-PERPETRATOR AND PARTICIPANT IN A CRIMINAL OFFENCE

## § 19

Perpetrator

(1) The perpetrator of a criminal offence is the person who committed the criminal offence himself.

(2) The perpetrator of a criminal offence may be a natural person and a legal person under the conditions established by a special regulation.

## § 20

Co-perpetrator

If a criminal offence was committed by joint conduct of two or more perpetrators (co-perpetrators), each of them is responsible as if he had committed the criminal offence himself.

## § 21

Participant

(1) A participant in a completed criminal offence or in its attempt is the person who intentionally

a) devised or directed the commission of the criminal offence (organizer),

b) induced another to commit the criminal offence (instigator),

c) requested another to commit the criminal offence (solicitor), or

d) provided assistance to another for the commission of the criminal offence, in particular by procuring means, removing obstacles, giving advice, reinforcing the intention, promising to assist after the criminal offence (aider).

(2) The provisions on criminal liability of the perpetrator shall apply to the criminal liability of a participant, unless this Act provides otherwise.

THIRD PART

CIRCUMSTANCES EXCLUDING CRIMINAL LIABILITY

## § 22

Age

(1) A person who at the time of committing an act otherwise criminal has not reached the age of fourteen is not criminally liable.

(2) For the criminal offence of sexual abuse under § 201, a person who at the time of committing the act has not reached the age of fifteen is not criminally liable.

## § 23

Insanity

A person who due to a mental disorder at the time of committing an act otherwise criminal could not recognize its unlawfulness or control his conduct is not criminally liable for this act, unless this Act provides otherwise.

FOURTH PART

CIRCUMSTANCES EXCLUDING THE UNLAWFULNESS OF AN ACT

## § 24

Necessity

(1) An act otherwise criminal by which someone averts a danger directly threatening an interest protected by this Act is not a criminal offence.

(2) It is not a case of necessity if it was possible to avert the danger directly threatening an interest protected by this Act under the given circumstances otherwise, or if the consequence caused is manifestly more serious than the one that was threatened. Likewise, it is not a case of necessity if the person to whom the danger directly threatened was obliged to endure it according to a generally binding legal regulation.

## § 25

Self-Defence

(1) An act otherwise criminal by which someone averts a directly threatening or continuing attack on an interest protected by this Act is not a criminal offence.

(2) It is not a case of self-defence if the defence was entirely manifestly disproportionate to the attack, in particular to its manner, place and time, circumstances relating to the person of the attacker or to the person of the defender.

(3) A person who averts an attack in the manner specified in paragraph 2 shall not be criminally liable if he acted in strong agitation caused by the attack, in particular as a result of confusion, fear or fright.

(4) If someone mistakenly believes, given the circumstances of the case, that an attack is threatening, this does not exclude criminal liability for an act committed negligently if the mistake consists in negligence.

## § 26

Authorized Use of a Weapon

(1) Use of a weapon in accordance with the law is not a criminal offence.

(2) Use of a weapon in accordance with the law is also considered its use against another in one's dwelling for the protection of life, health or property, if a person unlawfully enters the dwelling or unlawfully remains in it and it is not a case of self-defence. This does not apply if death was intentionally caused to another thereby.

## § 27

Permitted Risk

(1) An act otherwise criminal is not a criminal offence if someone, in accordance with the achieved state of knowledge, performs a socially beneficial activity in the field of production and research, if the socially beneficial result expected from the performance of the act cannot be achieved without the risk of endangering an interest protected by this Act.

(2) It is not a case of permitted risk if the result towards which the act is directed entirely manifestly does not correspond to the degree of risk, or the performance of the act contradicts a generally binding legal regulation, public interest, principles of humanity or is contrary to good morals.

## § 28

Exercise of Right and Duty

(1) An otherwise criminal act is not a criminal offense if it is the exercise of a right or duty arising from a generally binding legal regulation, from a decision of a court or other public authority, from the performance of work or other tasks, or from a contract that does not contradict or circumvent a generally binding legal regulation; the manner of exercising rights and duties must not contradict a generally binding legal regulation.

(2) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if the criminal offense of genocide under § 418, the criminal offense of involuntary disappearance under § 420a, or the criminal offense of inhumanity under § 425 was committed by fulfilling an order, instruction, command, or directive of an executive authority or superior.

(3) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if the criminal offense of war crime under § 433 was committed by fulfilling an order, instruction, command, or directive of an executive authority or superior, except where the person who fulfilled such order, instruction, command, or directive,

a) had a legal duty to fulfill such order, instruction, command, or directive,

b) did not know that such order, instruction, command, or directive was unlawful, and

c) the content of such order, instruction, command, or directive did not indicate that it was unlawful.

## § 29

Consent of the Injured Party

(1) An otherwise criminal act is not a criminal offense if it was performed with the consent of the injured party and is not directed against their life or health.

(2) It is not the consent of the injured party if the consent was not given in advance, was not serious and voluntary, or if another criminal offense was committed in connection with it.

(3) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if, according to the elements of the criminal offense, the act is to be criminal even when the consent of the injured party was given under paragraph 1.

## § 30

Performance of Agent's Task

(1) An otherwise criminal act is not a criminal offense if an agent appointed under a special regulation, in the course of detecting a criminal offense and identifying its perpetrator, endangers or violates an interest protected by this law only because they were forced to do so by a criminal group or terrorist group in which they operate, or if they commit such an act in justified fear for their own life or health or that of a close person.

(2) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if the agent commits the criminal offense of premeditated murder under § 144, murder under § 145, rape under § 199, sexual violence under § 200, sexual abuse under § 201, general endangerment under § 284 para. 2 to 4, endangering the safety of an aircraft and ship under § 291, hijacking an aircraft abroad under § 293, high treason under § 311, conspiracy against the Slovak Republic under § 312, terror under § 313, § 314, subversion under § 315, § 316, sabotage under § 317, espionage under § 318, genocide under § 418, terrorist attack under § 419, certain forms of participation in terrorism under § 419b, financing of terrorism under § 419c, travel for the purpose of terrorism under § 419d, or inhumanity under § 425, or if by the act referred to in paragraph 1 they cause serious injury to health or death.

(3) An otherwise criminal act referred to in § 332 to 334 and in § 336 para. 2 committed for the purpose of detecting a criminal offense or identifying the perpetrator of a criminal offense under § 326, § 328 to 330, § 336 para. 1, § 336a, or § 336b in the manner established in the Criminal Procedure Code is not a criminal offense.

SECOND PART

SANCTIONS

First Division

Types of Sanctions

## § 31

Punishment and Protective Measure

(1) Sanctions under this law are punishments and protective measures that are the legal consequence of a committed criminal offense or otherwise criminal act.

(2) Punishment is harm to the personal freedom, property, or other rights of the convicted person, which may be imposed on the perpetrator only by a court under this law for a committed criminal offense.

(3) A protective measure is harm to the personal freedom or property of the convicted person or another person, which may be imposed only by a court under this law in the interest of protecting society from criminal offenses or otherwise criminal acts.

## § 32

Types of Punishments

For committed criminal offenses, the court may impose on a perpetrator who is a natural person only

a) imprisonment,

b) house arrest,

c) community service,

d) fine,

e) forfeiture of property

f) forfeiture of a thing,

g) prohibition of activity,

h) prohibition of residence,

i) prohibition of participation in public events,

j) loss of honorary titles and decorations,

k) loss of military and other rank,

l) expulsion.

## § 33

Types of Protective Measures Protective measures are:

- a) protective treatment,
- b) protective education,
- c) protective supervision,
- d) detention,
- e) confiscation of a thing,
- f) confiscation of part of property.

Second Division

Basic Principles of Imposing Sanctions

## § 34

Principles of Imposing Punishments

(1) Punishment shall ensure the protection of society from the perpetrator by preventing them from committing further criminal activity and creating conditions for their education to lead a proper life and at the same time deter others from committing criminal offenses; punishment also expresses the moral condemnation of the perpetrator by society.

(2) Only such type of punishment and only to such extent as established in this law may be imposed on the perpetrator, while this law in its special part establishes only sentencing rates for imprisonment.

(3) Punishment shall affect only the perpetrator, so as to ensure the least possible impact on their family and close persons.

(4) In determining the type of punishment and its extent, the court shall take into account in particular the manner of committing the act and its consequence, culpability, motive, aggravating circumstances, mitigating circumstances, and the person of the perpetrator, their circumstances, and the possibility of their correction. In determining the type of punishment and its extent, the court shall also take into account that the perpetrator of the criminal offense obtained property benefit from the criminal offense; if the perpetrator's property or personal circumstances do not prevent it or it will not be to the detriment of compensation for damage, the court shall impose on them, taking into account the amount of this property benefit, in addition to another punishment, also some punishment that affects them in property, unless such punishment is imposed as independent upon fulfillment of the conditions under paragraph 6.

(5) In determining the type of punishment and its extent, the court shall take into account

a) in the case of co-perpetrators, also the extent to which the conduct of each of them contributed to the commission of the criminal offense,

b) in the case of an organizer, procurer, instigator, and accomplice, also the significance and nature of their participation in the committed criminal offense,

c) in the case of preparation for a felony and attempted criminal offense, also the extent to which the perpetrator's conduct approached the completion of the criminal offense, as well as the circumstances and reasons for which the completion of the criminal offense did not occur.

(6) The punishments referred to in § 32 may be imposed independently or several of these punishments may be imposed concurrently. For a criminal offense whose upper limit of the sentencing rate for imprisonment established in the special part of the law exceeds five years, the court must impose imprisonment unless this law provides otherwise.

(7) The following punishments cannot be imposed concurrently

a) imprisonment and house arrest,

b) imprisonment and community service,

c) fine and forfeiture of property,

d) forfeiture of a thing and forfeiture of property,

e) prohibition of residence and expulsion.

## § 35

Principles of imposing protective measures

(1) A protective measure may be imposed on the perpetrator of a criminal offense either in addition to a punishment, or even when waiving the punishment of the perpetrator, if the protective measure ensures the protection of society from the perpetrator more effectively than punishment.

(2) A protective measure may affect a person only in such a way as to ensure the least impact on their family and close persons.

(3) A protective measure may also be imposed on the perpetrator of an otherwise punishable act or another person, if it is necessary to ensure the protection of society from committing new criminal offenses.

(4) Protective supervision cannot be imposed in addition to protective education.

(5) When imposing protective measures, the court is not guided by the principle of proportionality to the offense committed, but by the need to protect society, taking into account also the need for treatment, education or completion of the correction of the perpetrator or another person. When imposing a protective measure, the court shall also take into account that the perpetrator of the criminal offense or another person has obtained property benefit from the criminal offense; if the property or personal circumstances of the perpetrator or another person do not prevent it, the court shall impose on them, taking into account the amount of this property benefit, a protective measure by which it affects their property.

(6) The execution of a protective measure must end at the latest upon achieving its purpose, or upon expiration of the period for which it was imposed, or upon reaching the age of the convicted person or another person established by law.

Mitigating circumstances and aggravating circumstances

## § 36

A mitigating circumstance is that the perpetrator

a) committed a criminal offense in justified strong emotional disturbance,

b) committed a criminal offense as a result of lack of knowledge or experience,

c) committed a criminal offense in connection with negative consequences of their illness,

d) committed a criminal offense at an age close to the age of juveniles or as an elderly person, if this fact had an impact on their mental or volitional capacity,

e) committed a criminal offense under pressure of dependency or subordination,

f) committed a criminal offense under the influence of threat or coercion,

g) committed a criminal offense as a result of distress that they did not cause themselves,

h) committed a criminal offense under the influence of distressing personal circumstances or family circumstances that they did not cause themselves,

i) committed a criminal offense averting an attack or other danger or acting under circumstances that, if further conditions were met, would otherwise exclude the criminal nature of the act, but acted without the conditions of necessary defense, extreme necessity, exercise of rights and duties or consent of the injured party, authorized use of weapons, permissible risk or performance of agent duties being fully met,

j) led a proper life before committing the criminal offense,

k) contributed to removing the harmful consequences of the criminal offense or voluntarily compensated the damage caused,

l) confessed to committing the criminal offense and sincerely regretted the criminal offense,

m) reported the criminal offense to the relevant authorities themselves,

n) assisted the relevant authorities in investigating criminal activity, or

o) contributed to the detection or conviction of an organized group, criminal group or terrorist group.

## § 37

An aggravating circumstance is that the perpetrator

a) committed a criminal offense from a particularly reprehensible motive,

b) committed a criminal offense as retaliation against another for fulfilling an obligation arising from law or other generally binding legal regulation towards the perpetrator, especially against a pedagogical employee or professional employee,

c) committed a criminal offense in order to thwart or make it difficult for another to exercise their fundamental rights and freedoms, or in order to facilitate or conceal another criminal offense,

d) committed a criminal offense during a natural disaster or other extraordinary event seriously threatening the life or health of people, other fundamental rights and freedoms, constitutional system, property, public order or morality,

e) abused their employment, profession, function or position to achieve an unjustified or disproportionate advantage,

f) committed a criminal offense publicly,

g) committed a criminal offense in a place enjoying special protection according to a generally binding legal regulation, especially in another person's house or apartment,

h) committed multiple criminal offenses,

i) abused a person who is not criminally responsible to commit a criminal offense,

j) incited a juvenile to commit a criminal offense,

k) committed a criminal offense as an organizer,

l) committed a criminal offense in connection with a foreign power or foreign agent,

m) was already convicted of a criminal offense; the court may, depending on the nature of the previous conviction, disregard this circumstance,

n) committed a criminal offense as a member of a group of persons while moving to the venue of a public event or from the venue of a public event, or

o) committed a criminal offense due to affiliation with a sports club.

## § 38

(1) A circumstance that is a statutory element of a criminal offense cannot be considered as a mitigating circumstance, aggravating circumstance, circumstance that conditions the imposition of a sentence below the statutory lower limit of the sentence range, or circumstance that conditions the application of a higher sentence range.

(2) When determining the type of sentence and its extent, the court must take into account the ratio and degree of seriousness of mitigating circumstances and aggravating circumstances.

(3) If the ratio of mitigating circumstances prevails, the upper limit of the statutory sentence range is reduced by one third.

(4) If the ratio of aggravating circumstances prevails, the lower limit of the statutory sentence range is increased by one third.

(5) For repeated commission of a felony, the lower limit of the statutory sentence range is increased by one half; in such case, the provision of paragraph 4 shall not apply.

(6) For repeated commission of a particularly serious felony, the lower limit of the statutory sentence range is increased by two thirds; in such case, the provisions of paragraphs 4 and 5 shall not apply.

(7) The provisions of paragraphs 4 to 6 shall not apply if an increased aggregate sentence or aggregate sentence is imposed simultaneously according to § 41(2) or according to § 42, if the simultaneous application of these provisions would be disproportionately severe for the perpetrator.

(8) The reduction of the upper limit or increase of the lower limit of the sentence range according to paragraphs 3 to 6 shall be carried out only within the statutory sentence range; the basis for reducing or increasing the sentence range is the difference between the upper and lower limits of the statutory sentence range. The reduction of the upper limit or increase of the lower limit of the statutory sentence range shall not apply in cases where the special part of the law establishes only a sentence of imprisonment for twenty-five years or life imprisonment.

## § 39

Extraordinary Reduction of Punishment

(1) If the court, considering the circumstances of the case or the offender's situation, finds that applying the sentence established by this Act would be disproportionately severe for the offender and that a sentence of shorter duration is sufficient to ensure the protection of society, the offender may be sentenced below the lower limit of the sentence established by this Act.

(2) The court may reduce the sentence below the lower limit of the sentence established by this Act also when sentencing an offender

a) for preparation of a felony or for an attempt to commit a criminal offense and if, considering the nature and severity of the preparation or attempt, the court finds that applying the sentence established by this Act would be disproportionately severe for the offender and that a sentence of shorter duration is sufficient for the protection of society,

b) who has significantly contributed to clarifying a criminal offense committed in favor of a criminal group or terrorist group, or helped to prevent the commission of a criminal offense that another person was preparing or attempting to commit in favor of a criminal group or terrorist group, by reporting their activity to law enforcement authorities and providing them with information they would not otherwise have obtained, thereby helping them to prevent or mitigate the consequences of the criminal offense, identify or convict the offenders, or secure evidence of the criminal offense in favor of convicting the criminal group or terrorist group,

c) who committed a criminal offense in a state of diminished responsibility, and the court finds that, considering the offender's health condition, it would be possible to achieve the protection of society with a sentence of shorter duration while simultaneously imposing protective treatment, whereby the court is not bound by the limitations set out in paragraph 3 and simultaneously imposes protective treatment,

d) in proceedings on an agreement on admission of guilt and acceptance of punishment, or

e) who has contributed to an exceptionally significant degree to clarifying a criminal offense of corruption under Chapter Eight of Part Three of the Special Part of this Act, a criminal offense of establishing, promoting and supporting a criminal group under § 296, a criminal offense of establishing, promoting and supporting a terrorist group under § 297, or a particularly serious felony committed by an organized group, criminal group or terrorist group, or to identifying or convicting its offender by providing evidence of such an offense in criminal proceedings, if, considering the nature and severity of the criminal offense committed by them, the court finds that the purpose of punishment can be achieved with a sentence of shorter duration; a sentence of imprisonment may not be reduced below the lower limit of the sentence where the person is an organizer, instigator or client of the criminal offense about which they provided evidence in criminal proceedings.

(3) When imposing a sentence below the statutory sentence, however, the court may not impose

a) a sentence of imprisonment shorter than twenty years if the offender is convicted of a criminal offense of premeditated murder under § 144 para. 3, genocide under § 418 para. 3, terrorist attack under § 419 para. 2, crimes against humanity under § 425 para. 2, or war crimes under § 433 para. 2,

b) a sentence of imprisonment shorter than eight years if in the Special Part of this Act the lower limit of the sentence of imprisonment is at least fifteen years,

c) a sentence of imprisonment shorter than five years if in the Special Part of this Act the lower limit of the sentence of imprisonment is at least ten years,

d) a sentence of imprisonment shorter than two years if in the Special Part of this Act the lower limit of the sentence of imprisonment is at least five years,

e) a sentence of imprisonment shorter than six months if in the Special Part of this Act the lower limit of the sentence of imprisonment is less than five years, and

f) a sentence of prohibition of activity, prohibition of residence and expulsion for a period shorter than six months.

(4) In proceedings on an agreement on admission of guilt and acceptance of punishment, the court may impose a sentence of imprisonment reduced by one third below the lower limit of the statutory sentence, and in the case of criminal offenses specified in paragraph 3 point a) a sentence of imprisonment not shorter than twenty years.

## § 40

Waiver of Punishment

(1) Punishment of an offender for a misdemeanor may be waived, if the misdemeanor did not cause death or serious bodily harm, if

a) the offender admitted to committing the misdemeanor, regrets having committed it and shows effective efforts towards reform, and if, considering the nature of the misdemeanor committed and the offender's previous life, it can be reasonably expected that the mere hearing of the case before the court will be sufficient for their reform,

b) the court accepts a guarantee for the offender's reform and finds that, considering the educational influence of the person who offered the guarantee, the nature of the misdemeanor committed and the person of the offender, imposing a sentence is not necessary, or

c) the misdemeanor was committed in a state of diminished responsibility and the court finds that protective treatment, which it simultaneously imposes, will ensure the protection of society and the offender's reform more effectively than punishment; this does not apply if the state of diminished responsibility was caused by the influence of an addictive substance.

(2) Punishment of an offender for a criminal offense may be waived if the criminal offense was committed by a person under duress in direct connection with being a victim of a criminal offense of human trafficking under § 179, a criminal offense of sexual abuse under § 201 to 202, a criminal offense of abuse of a close person and entrusted person under § 208, or a criminal offense of production of child pornography under § 368.

(3) If punishment of the offender has been waived under paragraph 1 or 2, the offender shall be regarded as if they had not been convicted.

## § 40a

Conditional Waiver of Punishment

(1) The court may conditionally waive punishment of an offender for a misdemeanor under § 171 para. 1 or 2 if it simultaneously imposes protective treatment under the conditions set out in § 73.

(2) The court shall determine a probationary period of up to two years for the offender; the probationary period begins to run on the day following the day on which the decision on conditional waiver of punishment becomes final.

(3) If the offender whose punishment has been conditionally waived has complied with the conditions of protective treatment, the court shall declare that they have proven themselves; otherwise, it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, on imposing a sentence.

(4) If the court has not made a decision under paragraph 3 within one year from the expiry of the probationary period without the offender being at fault for this, the offender shall be deemed to have proven themselves.

(5) If the court has declared that the offender whose punishment has been conditionally waived has proven themselves, or if they are deemed to have proven themselves, they shall be regarded as if they had not been convicted.

## § 41

Aggregate Sentence and Joint Sentence

(1) If the court sentences an offender for two or more criminal offences, it shall impose an aggregate sentence according to the statutory provision that applies to the most severely punishable criminal offence among them. In addition to the punishment permissible under such statutory provision, another type of punishment may be imposed within the aggregate sentence if its imposition would be justified by any of the concurrent criminal offences. If the lower limits of the penalty ranges for imprisonment are different, the lower limit of the aggregate sentence is the highest of them.

(2) If the court imposes an aggregate sentence of imprisonment for two or more intentional criminal offences, of which at least one is a felony, committed by two or more acts, the upper limit of the penalty range of imprisonment for the most severely punishable criminal offence among them is increased by one third. The upper limit of the increased penalty range shall not exceed twenty-five years and for juveniles the penalty range specified in § 117 para. 1 or 3. In addition to imprisonment, another type of punishment may be imposed within the aggregate sentence if its imposition would be justified by any of the adjudicated criminal offences.

(3) If the court sentences an offender for another partial attack which forms part of a continuing criminal offence, for another partial attack of which a conviction judgment was pronounced by a first instance court that has already become final, the court shall cancel in the judgment the earlier verdict of guilt concerning the continuing criminal offence and the criminal offences committed with it in real concurrence, the entire verdict on punishment, as well as other verdicts that have their basis in the stated verdict of guilt. The court, bound by the factual findings in the cancelled judgment, shall decide again on the guilt for the continuing criminal offence including the new partial attack, or for criminal offences committed with it in real concurrence, as well as on the joint sentence for the continuing criminal offence, which shall not be more lenient than the punishment imposed by the earlier judgment. The court shall also decide on subsequent verdicts that have their basis in the verdict of guilt. If punishment is imposed for multiple criminal offences, the provisions of paragraphs 1 and 2, § 42 and § 43 shall be applied accordingly.

(4) The provision of paragraph 3 shall not apply if the previous judgment was issued by a court of another member state of the European Union.

## § 42

Cumulative Sentence

(1) If the court sentences an offender for a criminal offence which he committed before a conviction judgment for another criminal offence of his was pronounced by a first instance court, it shall impose a cumulative sentence according to the principles for imposing an aggregate sentence.

(2) Together with the imposition of a cumulative sentence, the court shall cancel the verdict on punishment imposed on the offender by the earlier judgment, as well as all other decisions substantively following this verdict, if, due to the change that occurred by the cancellation, they lost their basis. The cumulative sentence shall not be more lenient than the punishment imposed by the earlier judgment. Within the cumulative sentence, the court shall impose the punishment of deprivation of honorary titles and decorations, the punishment of deprivation of military and other rank, the punishment of forfeiture of property, pecuniary punishment, the punishment of forfeiture of a thing, the punishment of prohibition of activity or the punishment of prohibition of participation in public events, if such punishment was already imposed by the earlier judgment and if the provision of § 34 para. 7 does not prevent it.

(3) The provision on cumulative sentence shall not apply if the earlier conviction is of such nature that the offender is regarded as if he had not been convicted or if the earlier judgment was issued by a court of another member state of the European Union.

## § 43

Additional Sentence

If the court sentences an offender for a criminal offence which he committed before the punishment imposed by an earlier judgment was executed, and imposes on him a punishment of the same type, this punishment together with the not yet executed part of the punishment imposed by the earlier judgment shall not exceed the maximum measure allowed by this law for this type of punishment. If one of these punishments is imprisonment, such maximum measure means a period of twenty-five years or life imprisonment.

## § 44

Waiver of Cumulative Sentence and Additional Sentence

The court shall waive the imposition of a cumulative sentence under § 42 or the imposition of an additional sentence under § 43, if it considers the punishment imposed by the earlier judgment sufficient for the protection of society and correction of the offender.

## § 45

Crediting of Detention and Punishment

(1) If criminal prosecution was conducted against the offender in detention and his conviction occurs in this proceeding, the time spent in detention shall be credited toward the imposed punishment, or toward the aggregate sentence or cumulative sentence, if crediting is possible with regard to the type of imposed punishment. The same procedure shall be followed if the court waives the imposition of a cumulative sentence or additional sentence.

(2) If the offender was apprehended during criminal prosecution and his conviction occurs in this proceeding, the period of restriction of personal freedom shall be credited toward the imposed punishment, if crediting is possible with regard to the type of imposed punishment.

(3) If the offender was punished by a court or other body and his conviction occurred for the same act, the executed punishment shall be credited toward the imposed punishment, if crediting is possible with regard to the type of imposed punishment. The court shall proceed in the same way if it imposed an aggregate sentence or cumulative sentence on the offender.

(4) If crediting of detention or punishment under paragraphs 1 to 3 is not possible, the court shall take this fact into account when determining the type of punishment, or the measure of punishment.

(5) According to the same principles, the court shall also proceed in the case that the offender was in detention abroad or was punished by a body of a foreign state for the same act.

Third Part

Imposition and Execution of Individual Punishments

## § 46

Imprisonment

Imprisonment may be imposed as a sentence for a definite period, maximum of twenty-five years or as life imprisonment.

## § 47

Life Imprisonment

(1) Life imprisonment may be imposed by the court only for a criminal offence for which this law in the special part allows it, and only under the conditions that

a) the imposition of such punishment requires effective protection of society and

b) there is no hope that the offender could be corrected by imprisonment for a period up to twenty-five years.

(2) If the court sentences the offender for a completed criminal offence of premeditated murder under § 144, murder under § 145, bodily harm under § 155, unauthorised production of narcotic and psychotropic substances, poisons or precursors, their possession and trafficking in them under § 172 para. 5, 6, 7 or 8, trafficking in human beings under § 179, entrusting a child to the power of another under § 180 para. 2 or 3 or under § 181, hostage taking under § 185, abduction abroad under § 187, robbery under § 188, extortion under § 189 para. 2, 3 or 4, coercion under § 190 or § 191 para. 2, 3 or 4, rape under § 199, sexual violence under § 200, sexual abuse under § 201 para. 2, 3 or 4, abuse of a close person and entrusted person under § 208, general endangerment under § 284, endangering the safety of an aircraft and ship under § 291, abduction of an aircraft abroad under § 293, establishment, instigation and support of a criminal group under § 296, establishment, instigation and support of a terrorist group under § 297, terror under § 313 or § 314, violent crossing of a state border under § 354 para. 2, 3 or 4, people smuggling under § 355 para. 3, 4 or 5, production of child pornography under § 368, genocide under § 418, terrorist attack under § 419, certain forms of participation in terrorism under § 419b, financing of terrorism under § 419c, travel for the purpose of terrorism under § 419d or inhumanity under § 425, who has already been for such criminal offences, even at the stage of attempt, twice punished by unconditional imprisonment, it shall impose life imprisonment on him, if the conditions stated in paragraph 1 letters a) and b) are met; otherwise it shall impose imprisonment for twenty-five years, if circumstances worthy of special consideration do not prevent it. However, the court cannot impose imprisonment below twenty years on such offender.

## § 48

External differentiation of the execution of custodial sentence

(1) The custodial sentence is executed in a differentiated manner in institutions for the execution of custodial sentences (hereinafter referred to as "institution for execution of sentence") with minimum, medium or maximum security level.

(2) The court shall assign the offender to serve the custodial sentence as a rule to an institution for execution of sentence

a) with minimum security level, if in the last ten years before committing the criminal offense he/she was not serving a custodial sentence imposed on him/her for an intentional criminal offense,

b) with medium security level, if in the last ten years before committing the criminal offense he/she was serving a custodial sentence imposed on him/her for an intentional criminal offense; however, a previous conviction shall not be taken into account if the offender is regarded as if he/she had not been convicted.

(3) The court shall assign to an institution for execution of sentence with maximum security level

a) an offender to whom it has imposed a life sentence, or

b) an offender of a particularly serious felony.

(4) The court may assign an offender also to an institution for execution of sentence with a different security level than the one to which he/she should be assigned according to paragraph 2, if, having regard to the gravity of the criminal offense and the degree of the offender's disturbance, it considers that his/her rehabilitation will be better ensured in an institution for execution of sentence with a different security level. In this way, it may assign an offender to an institution for execution of sentence with minimum security level even if he/she was serving a sentence for an intentional misdemeanor in the last ten years before committing the criminal offense. However, it cannot assign to an institution for execution of sentence with minimum or medium security level an offender to whom a life sentence has been imposed, or an offender of a particularly serious felony to whom a custodial sentence exceeding fifteen years has been imposed.

(5) When assigning an offender to an institution for execution of sentence with minimum, medium or maximum security level, the court is not bound by the provisions of paragraphs 2 and 3 even when it approves a plea agreement or reduces the sentence below the lower limit of the sentencing range pursuant to § 39 para. 2 letter b).

Conditional suspension of execution of custodial sentence

## § 49

(1) The court may conditionally suspend the execution of a custodial sentence not exceeding two years if

a) having regard to the person of the offender, in particular taking into account his/her previous life and the environment in which he/she lives and works, and the circumstances of the case, it has reasonable grounds to believe that the execution of the custodial sentence is not necessary to ensure the protection of society and the rehabilitation of the offender, or

b) it accepts a guarantee for the rehabilitation of the offender and if, having regard to the educational influence of the person who offered the guarantee, it considers that the execution of the custodial sentence is not necessary.

(2) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if the court convicts the offender of an intentional criminal offense committed during the probationary period of conditional conviction or during the probationary period of conditional release from execution of custodial sentence.

## § 50

(1) When granting the conditional suspension of execution of custodial sentence, the court shall determine the probationary period from one year to five years. The probationary period begins to run on the day following the day on which the judgment becomes final. The probationary period does not run during the execution of an unconditional custodial sentence and during detention.

(2) In the context of granting the conditional suspension of execution of custodial sentence, the court may impose on the offender the obligation to compensate, during the probationary period and according to his/her abilities, for the damage caused by the criminal offense or to pay a debt or overdue maintenance.

(3) The probationary period that has already elapsed shall be credited towards the newly determined probationary period when granting the conditional suspension of execution of custodial sentence imposed for the same act, or towards the probationary period determined when imposing an aggregate sentence or joint sentence.

(4) If the convicted person has led a proper life during the probationary period and has fulfilled the obligation to compensate for the damage caused by the criminal offense or to pay a debt or overdue maintenance, if imposed, the court shall declare that he/she has proven himself/herself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the custodial sentence shall be executed. The court shall proceed in the same manner also in the case of other restrictions or obligations pursuant to § 51 para. 3 and 4, if imposed. If the convicted person is serving a custodial sentence, the court shall decide so that the executions of custodial sentences follow each other continuously. Exceptionally, the court may, having regard to the circumstances of the case, maintain the conditional conviction in force even though the convicted person by conduct committed during the probationary period has given cause for ordering the execution of the sentence, and at the same time may

a) establish probation supervision over the convicted person and impose hitherto unimposed reasonable restrictions or reasonable obligations set out in § 51 para. 3 and 4 aimed at ensuring that he/she leads a proper life,

b) reasonably extend the probationary period, but not by more than two years, and must not exceed the upper limit of the probationary period established in paragraph 1.

(5) If the court has not made a decision pursuant to paragraph 4 within one year from the expiry of the probationary period without the convicted person being at fault, it shall be deemed that the convicted person has proven himself/herself.

(6) Likewise, it shall be deemed that the convicted person has proven himself/herself if the court has not made a decision pursuant to paragraph 4 within two years from the expiry of the probationary period without the convicted person being at fault in the event that criminal proceedings are being conducted against the convicted person within this period for another criminal offense committed during the probationary period.

(7) If it has been declared that the convicted person has proven himself/herself during the probationary period, or if it is deemed that he/she has proven himself/herself, he/she shall be regarded as if he/she had not been convicted.

(8) If the court decides that the custodial sentence which was conditionally suspended shall be executed, it shall also decide on the manner of execution of the sentence.

Conditional suspension of execution of custodial sentence with probation supervision

## § 51

(1) The court may, under the conditions specified in § 49 paragraph 1, conditionally suspend the execution of a custodial sentence not exceeding three years if it simultaneously imposes probation supervision over the offender's conduct during the probationary period. The provision of § 49 paragraph 2 applies equally.

(2) When determining probation supervision, the court shall set the probationary period from one year to five years. The probationary period begins to run on the day following the day on which the judgment becomes final. At the same time, the court shall impose restrictions or obligations that are part of probation supervision. The probationary period does not run during the execution of an unconditional custodial sentence and during detention.

(3) Restrictions consist in particular of a prohibition on

a) participation in specified public events,

b) consumption of alcoholic beverages and other addictive substances,

c) meeting with persons who have a negative influence on the offender or who were his accomplices or participants in the criminal offense,

d) entering designated places or premises where he committed the criminal offense,

e) participation in gambling, playing on gaming machines and making bets,

f) contact with a specified person in any form including contact through electronic communication service or other similar means.

(4) Obligations consist in particular of an order to

a) not approach a specified person closer than five meters and not stay near the residence of a specified person or in a specified place where such person stays or visits.

b) stay at a specified address at a specified time,

c) move out of an apartment or house in which he is staying unlawfully or which he has unlawfully occupied,

d) compensate for damage caused during the probationary period,

e) pay a debt or overdue alimony during the probationary period,

f) personally or publicly apologize to the injured party,

g) obtain certain work qualifications or participate in a retraining course during the probationary period,

h) undergo a social training program or other educational program in cooperation with a probation and mediation officer or other specialist,

i) undergo treatment for addiction to addictive substances if he has not been imposed protective treatment,

j) undergo psychotherapy or participate in psychological counseling during the probationary period,

k) become employed during the probationary period or demonstrably seek employment,

l) appear at a specified time at the relevant unit of the Police Force determined according to the place of residence, in justified cases also repeatedly.

(5) An offender who has been imposed probation supervision is obliged to tolerate supervision carried out by a probation and mediation officer and to submit to supervision by technical means if such supervision is ordered; ordering supervision by technical means is possible if the conditions under a special regulation are met.

(6) The probationary period under paragraph 2 does not apply to the execution of other sentences imposed in addition to probation supervision.

(7) The probationary period that has already elapsed is counted towards the probationary period that was determined when imposing new probation supervision imposed for the same act, or towards the probationary period determined when imposing an aggregate sentence or a joint sentence.

(8) When imposing a restriction under paragraph 3 letter f) and an obligation under paragraph 4 letter a), the court shall inform the specified person about the possibility to request the issuing of a European protection order for the purpose of ensuring their protection in the territory of another Member State of the European Union according to a special regulation.

(9) The obligation under paragraph 4 letter h) may be imposed after previously requesting a report from the probation and mediation officer on the appropriateness of its imposition, if the appropriateness of imposing this obligation does not result from an expert opinion or expert assessment.

## § 52

(1) If the convicted person led a proper life during the probationary period, complied with the conditions of probation supervision and fulfilled the imposed restrictions and obligations, the court shall declare that he has proven himself; otherwise it shall order an unconditional custodial sentence, possibly already during the probationary period. Exceptionally, the court may, with regard to the circumstances of the case, keep probation supervision in force even though the convicted person by his conduct committed during the probationary period gave reason for ordering the execution of the sentence, and at the same time may

a) appropriately extend probation supervision, but not more than by two years, and must not exceed the upper limit of probation supervision established in § 51 paragraph 2,

b) impose previously unimposed appropriate restrictions or obligations, or

c) order supervision of imposed appropriate restrictions or obligations by technical means, if the conditions under a special regulation are met and if such supervision has not yet been ordered.

(2) If the court does not make a decision under paragraph 1 within one year from the expiration of the probationary period without the convicted person being at fault for this, it is deemed that the convicted person has proven himself.

(3) It is also deemed that the convicted person has proven himself if the court does not make a decision under paragraph 1 within two years from the expiration of the probationary period without the convicted person being at fault for this in the event that criminal prosecution is being conducted against the convicted person during this period for another criminal offense committed during the probationary period.

(4) If it has been declared that the convicted person has proven himself during the probationary period, or if it is deemed that he has proven himself, he is regarded as if he had not been convicted.

(5) If the court converts a conditional custodial sentence into an unconditional custodial sentence, it shall simultaneously decide on the manner of execution of the sentence.

## § 53

Sentence of house arrest

(1) The court may impose a sentence of house arrest up to four years on an offender of a criminal offense if

a) with regard to the nature of the criminal offense specified in paragraph 2, the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of this sentence is sufficient,

b) the offender has given a written promise that at a specified time he will stay in a residence at a specified address and will provide necessary cooperation during the execution of supervision,

c) the conditions for execution of supervision by technical means are met.

(2) The court may impose a sentence of house arrest in the amount according to paragraph 1 for a criminal offense with an upper limit of the penalty range established by this Act not exceeding ten years, but at least at the lower limit of the penalty range for custodial sentence established by this Act.

(3) The convicted person during the execution of the sentence of house arrest is obliged, at the time determined by the court, to stay in his residence including the outdoor spaces belonging to it, to lead a proper life and to submit to supervision by technical means.

(4) The court may impose on the offender during the execution of the sentence of house arrest restrictions or obligations specified in § 51 paragraphs 3 and 4 aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life.

(5) During the execution of the sentence of house arrest, the convicted person may leave his residence only with prior consent of the probation and mediation officer, and only for a pressing reason and for the time strictly necessary. This time is counted towards the execution of the sentence.

(6) If the convicted person does not fulfill the restrictions or obligations arising from the sentence of house arrest or frustrates the execution of supervision by technical means, the court shall convert the sentence into an unconditional custodial sentence so that one unexecuted day of house arrest equals one day of unconditional custodial sentence, and shall simultaneously decide on the manner of execution of this sentence.

Sentence of mandatory work

## § 54

The court may impose a sentence of mandatory work with the consent of the offender in the amount from 40 to 300 hours if it convicts him for a misdemeanor for which the law allows the imposition of a custodial sentence whose upper limit of the custodial sentence range does not exceed five years.

## § 55

(1) The convicted person is obliged to execute the sentence of mandatory work no later than one year from the order of execution of this sentence. The court may also impose on the offender for this period appropriate restrictions or obligations referred to in § 51 paragraphs 3 and 4, aimed at leading a proper life, and shall usually impose on him the obligation to compensate, according to his means and abilities, for the damage caused by the criminal offense. The following periods are not included in the period of execution of the sentence of mandatory work:

a) the convicted person could not execute the sentence of mandatory work due to temporary incapacity for work or because work was not assigned to him during this period,

b) performed compulsory military service or other service instead of compulsory military service,

c) stayed abroad,

d) was in custody or serving a sentence of imprisonment in another case.

(2) The court shall not impose a sentence of mandatory work if the offender is long-term incapacitated for work or disabled.

(3) The convicted person is obliged to execute the sentence of mandatory work personally and in his free time without entitlement to remuneration.

(4) If the convicted person during the execution of the sentence of mandatory work did not lead a proper life or culpably failed to perform the work to the specified extent, or failed to comply with the imposed restrictions or obligations, the court shall convert the sentence of mandatory work or its remainder into a sentence of imprisonment so that for every two hours of work not performed it shall order one day of unconditional imprisonment and at the same time decide on the manner of execution of this sentence.

(5) The court may waive the execution of the sentence of mandatory work if the convicted person during the execution of this sentence, through no fault of his own, became permanently incapacitated for work or disabled, or for other serious reasons.

Monetary Penalty

## § 56

(1) The court may impose a monetary penalty from 160 euros to 33,930 euros on the offender of an intentional criminal offense by which he obtained or attempted to obtain a pecuniary benefit.

(2) Without fulfilling the conditions referred to in paragraph 1, the court may impose a monetary penalty if it is imposing it for a misdemeanor and, given the nature of the misdemeanor committed and the possibility of reforming the offender, it does not impose a sentence of imprisonment.

(3) If justified by the amount of the monetary penalty imposed and the personal and property circumstances of the offender, the court may decide that the convicted person shall pay the monetary penalty in monthly installments. At the same time, it shall determine their amount and a period of no more than one year from the entry into force of the conviction judgment within which the monetary penalty must be paid.

(4) A monetary penalty already served by the convicted person shall be credited towards a new monetary penalty imposed on the offender for the same act, or towards this penalty if it was imposed as a cumulative sentence or aggregate sentence.

(5) The court shall not impose a monetary penalty if it would thwart the possibility of compensating for the damage caused by the criminal offense.

## § 57

(1) When imposing a monetary penalty, the court shall take into account the personal and property circumstances of the offender. It shall not impose a monetary penalty if it is clear that the convicted person will not be able to pay it.

(2) The paid amount of the monetary penalty belongs to the state.

(3) If the court imposes a monetary penalty, it shall establish, in case the execution of the monetary penalty could be intentionally thwarted, a substitute sentence of imprisonment of up to five years. The substitute sentence together with the imposed sentence of imprisonment must not exceed the legally permitted limit of the sentencing range.

(4) If the substitute sentence would exceed the limit referred to in paragraph 3, or if the monetary penalty is imposed alongside a sentence of life imprisonment, the court shall not impose a substitute sentence.

Forfeiture of Property

## § 58

(1) The court may impose a sentence of forfeiture of property, given the circumstances of the criminal offense committed and the circumstances of the offender, if it sentences the offender to life imprisonment or if it sentences him to an unconditional sentence of imprisonment for a particularly serious felony by which the offender obtained or attempted to obtain a pecuniary benefit of large scale or by which he caused damage of large scale.

(2) The court shall impose a sentence of forfeiture of property without fulfilling the conditions referred to in paragraph 1 if it convicts the offender of committing the criminal offense of unlawful production of narcotics and psychotropic substances, poisons or precursors, their possession and trafficking in them pursuant to § 173 paragraph 3, the criminal offense of trafficking in human beings pursuant to § 179, the criminal offense of entrusting a child to the power of another pursuant to § 180 paragraph 2 or 3 or § 181, the criminal offense of extortion pursuant to § 189 paragraph 2 letter c), the criminal offense of gross coercion pursuant to § 190 paragraph 1, 3, 4 or 5 or § 191 paragraph 3 or 4, the criminal offense of coercion pursuant to § 192 paragraph 3 or 4, the criminal offense of unauthorized operation of lotteries and other similar games pursuant to § 230 paragraph 2, 3 or 4, the criminal offense of legalization of proceeds from criminal activity pursuant to § 234, the criminal offense of counterfeiting, alteration and unauthorized production of money and securities pursuant to § 270, the criminal offense of putting into circulation counterfeit, altered and unauthorizedly produced money and securities pursuant to § 271 paragraph 1, the criminal offense of production and possession of counterfeiting equipment pursuant to § 272 paragraph 3, the criminal offense of tax and insurance premium evasion pursuant to § 276 paragraph 3 or paragraph 4, the criminal offense of non-remittance of tax and insurance premium pursuant to § 277 paragraph 3 or paragraph 4, the criminal offense of tax fraud pursuant to § 277a paragraph 2 or paragraph 3, the criminal offense of non-payment of tax and insurance premium pursuant to § 278 paragraph 2 or 3, the criminal offense of violation of regulations on state technical measures for marking goods pursuant to § 279 paragraph 2 or 3, the criminal offense of terror pursuant to § 313 or § 314, the criminal offense of accepting a bribe pursuant to § 328 paragraph 2, the criminal offense of counterfeiting and alteration of public documents, official seals, official closures, official emblems and official marks pursuant to § 352 paragraph 6, the criminal offense of smuggling of migrants pursuant to § 355 or § 356, the criminal offense of procuring pursuant to § 367 paragraph 3, the criminal offense of production of child pornography pursuant to § 368, the criminal offense of distribution of child pornography pursuant to § 369, the criminal offense of endangering morality pursuant to § 372 paragraph 2 or 3, the criminal offense of terrorist attack pursuant to § 419, the criminal offense of certain forms of participation in terrorism pursuant to § 419b, the criminal offense of financing terrorism pursuant to § 419c or the criminal offense of travel for the purpose of terrorism pursuant to § 419d and the offender acquired property at least on a significant scale through criminal activity or from proceeds from criminal activity.

(3) The court shall impose a sentence of forfeiture of property without fulfilling the conditions referred to in paragraph 1 also when it convicts the offender of committing the criminal offense of unlawful production of narcotics and psychotropic substances, poisons or precursors, their possession and trafficking in them pursuant to § 172 paragraph 7 or 8 or § 173 paragraph 4, the criminal offense of legalization of proceeds from criminal activity pursuant to § 233 paragraph 3 or 4, the criminal offense of establishment, conspiracy and support of a criminal group pursuant to § 296, the criminal offense of establishment, conspiracy and support of a terrorist group pursuant to § 297, the criminal offense of accepting a bribe pursuant to § 328 paragraph 3 or § 329 paragraph 3 or the criminal offense of bribery pursuant to § 334 paragraph 2.

## § 59

(1) The sentence of forfeiture of property affects, to the extent belonging to the convicted person upon execution of the sentence of forfeiture of property after completion of bankruptcy proceedings,

a) the proceeds from the monetization of property,

b) property excluded from the inventory of the bankruptcy estate,

c) property subject to bankruptcy if monetization of the property did not occur.

(2) The state becomes the owner of the forfeited property, unless the court decides otherwise on the basis of a declared international treaty by which the Slovak Republic is bound.

(3) By a final court decision on forfeiture of property, the community property of spouses ceases to exist. Property in the debtor's community property of spouses belongs to the bankruptcy estate if it has not yet been divided. The division of community property of spouses that occurred earlier, including acts that changed its scope, may be contested in bankruptcy.

## § 60

Penalty of forfeiture of a thing

(1) The court shall impose the penalty of forfeiture of a thing,

a) which was used to commit a criminal offense,

b) which was intended for the commission of a criminal offense, or

c) which is proceeds from criminal activity.

(2) If the thing referred to in paragraph 1 is unattainable or unidentifiable, or is mixed with the property of the offender or with the property of another person acquired in accordance with the law, the court may impose forfeiture of a thing of such value that corresponds to the value of this thing.

(3) An unattainable thing means a thing destroyed, damaged, lost, stolen, made unusable, consumed, concealed, transferred to another person with the aim of removing it from the jurisdiction of authorities active in criminal proceedings or otherwise removed or saved costs.

(4) The court may impose the penalty of forfeiture of a thing only if it is a thing belonging to the offender.

(5) The state becomes the owner of the forfeited thing, unless the court decides otherwise on the basis of a promulgated international treaty by which the Slovak Republic is bound.

(6) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if

a) the injured party has a claim for compensation for damage arising from the act, the satisfaction of which would be prevented by the forfeiture of the thing,

b) the value of the thing is in obvious disproportion to the degree of seriousness of the misdemeanor, or

c) the court has waived the punishment of the offender.

## § 61

Penalty of prohibition of activity

(1) The penalty of prohibition of activity consists in prohibiting the convicted person for the duration of the execution of this penalty from performing a particular employment, profession or function or such activity for which special permission is required or the conditions of performance of which are regulated by a special regulation.

(2) The court may impose the penalty of prohibition of activity for one year to ten years if the offender committed a criminal offense in connection with this activity.

(3) If the court convicts an offender of the criminal offense of endangerment under the influence of an addictive substance according to § 289, which he committed as a driver of a means of transport, who has already been convicted of such a criminal offense or of the criminal offense of killing according to § 149 para. 4 or 5, which he committed as a driver of a means of transport, it shall impose on him the penalty of prohibition of activity in the upper half of the penalty rate specified in paragraph 2.

(4) The court may impose the penalty of prohibition of activity for life if it convicts the offender of the criminal offense of trafficking in human beings according to § 179, the criminal offense of rape according to § 199, the criminal offense of sexual violence according to § 200, the criminal offense of sexual abuse according to § 201 to 202, the criminal offense of production of child pornography according to § 368, the criminal offense of distribution of child pornography according to § 369 and the criminal offense of possession of child pornography and participation in a child pornographic performance according to § 370, if such a criminal offense was committed against a child.

(5) The court shall impose the penalty of prohibition of activity for life if it convicts the offender of a criminal offense

a) of endangerment under the influence of an addictive substance according to § 289, which he committed as a driver of a means of transport, although he has already been convicted twice of such a criminal offense, which he committed as a driver of a means of transport, or

b) of killing according to § 149 para. 4 or 5, which he committed as a driver of a means of transport.

(6) If the court convicts an offender of the criminal offense of tax and insurance reduction according to § 276 para. 2, 3 or para. 4, the criminal offense of non-remittance of tax and insurance according to § 277 para. 2, 3 or para. 4, the criminal offense of tax fraud according to § 277a para. 2 or para. 3, the criminal offense of non-payment of tax and insurance according to § 278 para. 2 or para. 3 and the criminal offense of frustrating the execution of tax administration according to § 278a para. 2 or para. 3, it shall impose on him the penalty of prohibition of activity in the upper half of the penalty rate specified in paragraph 2.

(7) The expungement of the conviction has no effect on the imposition of the penalty of prohibition of activity according to paragraphs 3 to 5.
