(8) The period of execution of an unconditional sentence of imprisonment and the period of execution of detention are not counted towards the period of execution of the penalty of prohibition of activity; however, the period during which the offender was deprived of the authorization for the activity that is the subject of the prohibition before the final judgment according to special regulations, and the period during which he was no longer allowed to perform this activity on the basis of a measure of a state authority, are counted.

(9) The penalty of prohibition of activity that the convicted person has already served is counted towards the new penalty of prohibition of activity that was imposed on the offender for the same act or towards this penalty if it was imposed as an aggregate penalty or a cumulative penalty.

(10) A final judgment imposing a penalty of prohibition of activity to perform the function of a member of a statutory body, a member of a supervisory body, a head of an organizational unit of an enterprise, a head of an enterprise of a foreign person, a head of an organizational unit of an enterprise of a foreign person or a procurator is, for the duration of the execution of the penalty, a decision on exclusion according to the provisions of commercial law.

## § 62

Penalty of prohibition of residence

(1) The penalty of prohibition of residence consists in the convicted person not being allowed to stay in a designated place or in a designated area for the duration of the execution of this penalty; permission is required for a temporary stay in such a place or in such an area in an essential personal matter.

(2) The court may impose the penalty of prohibition of residence for one year to five years for an intentional criminal offense if the protection of public order, family, health, morality or property requires it with regard to the offender's previous way of life and the place of commission of the act. The penalty of prohibition of residence cannot relate to the place or area in which the offender has permanent residence.

(3) The court may impose on the offender for the duration of the execution of this penalty appropriate restrictions and obligations specified in § 51 para. 3 and 4, aimed at leading a proper life, if it does not impose this penalty together with an unconditional sentence of imprisonment.

(4) The convicted person is obliged to submit to control by technical means if such control is ordered. Control by technical means may be ordered if the conditions according to a special regulation are met.

(5) The period of execution of a sentence of imprisonment is not counted towards the period of execution of the penalty of prohibition of residence.

(6) The penalty of prohibition of residence cannot be imposed together with the penalty of compulsory labor if the work is to be performed in a place where the convicted person is prohibited from staying.

## § 62a

Penalty of prohibition of attendance at public events

(1) The penalty of prohibition of attendance at public events consists in prohibiting the convicted person during the period of execution of this penalty from attending sports, cultural or other public events to the extent specified in the decision of the court.

(2) The court may impose the penalty of prohibition of attendance at public events for up to ten years if the offender commits an intentional criminal offense in connection with attendance at a public event or if the protection of public order, health, morality or property requires it with regard to the offender's previous way of life and the circumstances of the commission of the act.

(3) The penalty of prohibition of attendance at public events may be imposed as an independent penalty if, in view of the nature and seriousness of the misdemeanor committed, the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another penalty is not necessary.

(4) For the period of execution of the penalty of prohibition of attendance at public events or for a part thereof, the court may impose on the convicted person restrictions and obligations specified in § 51 para. 3 and 4, in particular to appear at a specified time at the relevant unit of the Police Force, and as a rule the court shall also impose on him to compensate, according to his means, for the damage caused by the criminal offense or the part thereof that the court shall also determine.

(5) The convicted person is obliged to submit to control by technical means if such control is ordered. Control by technical means may be ordered if the conditions according to a special regulation are met.

(6) The period of execution of an unconditional sentence of imprisonment is not counted towards the period of execution of the penalty of prohibition of attendance at public events; however, the period during which, in the same case, the offender was prohibited before the final judgment from attending public events that are the subject of the prohibition, is counted.

## § 63

Penalty of Loss of Honorary Titles and Decorations

(1) The penalty of loss of honorary titles and decorations consists in the convicted person losing decorations and other honorary titles awarded under special regulations.

(2) The court may impose the penalty of loss of honorary titles and decorations if it sentences the offender for a particularly serious felony to a sentence of imprisonment exceeding five years.

(3) The court may impose this penalty also in addition to a shorter sentence of imprisonment or in addition to another penalty if, having regard to the nature of the particularly serious felony committed, the protection of the dignity of the honorary title and decoration that were awarded to the offender before conviction so requires.

## § 64

Penalty of Loss of Military and Other Rank

(1) The penalty of loss of military and other rank consists in the convicted person's rank in the armed forces being reduced to the rank of soldier, and in the case of a member in a service relationship, his rank in that corps is taken away.

(2) The court shall impose the penalty of loss of military and other rank on an offender who holds such rank if it sentences him for an intentional criminal offense to a sentence of imprisonment of at least two years without conditional deferral of its execution.

(3) The court may impose this penalty also in addition to a shorter sentence of imprisonment or in addition to another penalty if it sentences an offender who is a member of the armed forces or a member in a service relationship, and if, having regard to the nature of the criminal offense committed, discipline and order in the armed forces or in service so requires.

## § 65

Penalty of Expulsion

(1) If the safety of persons or property or other public interest so requires, the court may impose on an offender who is not a citizen of the Slovak Republic nor a person who has been granted asylum or provided with subsidiary protection, the penalty of expulsion from the territory of the Slovak Republic.

(2) The penalty of expulsion cannot be imposed on an offender,

a) whose citizenship could not be determined or the state from which he arrived on the territory of the Slovak Republic,

b) who is a citizen of a Member State of the European Union or a citizen of a contracting state of the Agreement on the European Economic Area or his family member regardless of citizenship and has permanent residence on the territory of the Slovak Republic; this does not apply if such person represents a threat to state security or public order,

c) who is a citizen of a Member State of the European Union or a citizen of a contracting state of the Agreement on the European Economic Area with continuous residence on the territory of the Slovak Republic for more than ten years; this does not apply if such person represents a threat to state security,

d) who is a child and is a citizen of a Member State of the European Union or a citizen of a contracting state of the Agreement on the European Economic Area, if expulsion is not in the best interests of the child,

e) if he is to be expelled to a state in which his personal freedom would be threatened on grounds of his race, nationality, religion, membership in a particular social group or political opinion; this does not apply to one who can reasonably be considered dangerous to the security of the Slovak Republic, or to one who has been convicted of a particularly serious felony and represents a danger to the Slovak Republic or

f) if he is to be expelled to a state in which his life would be threatened on grounds of his race, nationality, religion, membership in a particular social group or political opinion, or in which he would face torture, cruel, inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment, or in which the death penalty has been imposed on him or there is a presumption that in ongoing criminal proceedings such penalty may be imposed on him.

(3) Family member under paragraph 2 letter b) is a family member under the regulations on the residence of foreigners.

(4) The court may impose the penalty of expulsion for one year to fifteen years.

Part Four

Conversion of the Remainder of a Sentence of Imprisonment to a Sentence of Home Imprisonment, Conditional Release and Conditional Waiver of Execution of the Remainder of Certain Penalties

## § 65a

Conversion of the Remainder of a Sentence of Imprisonment to a Sentence of Home Imprisonment

(1) The court may convert the remainder of a sentence of imprisonment to a sentence of home imprisonment for a person convicted of a misdemeanor if the conditions under § 53 para. 1 are met and at the same time

a) the convicted person has demonstrated improvement in the execution of the sentence by fulfilling his duties and by his conduct,

b) the convicted person has served one third of the imposed sentence of imprisonment or of the sentence of imprisonment mitigated by a decision of the President of the Slovak Republic,

c) the remainder of the unserved sentence of imprisonment does not exceed three years,

d) it does not concern the execution of a sentence that was ordered after a decision on failure to prove oneself during the probationary period determined in the case of conditional deferral of execution of a sentence of imprisonment, conditional deferral of execution of a sentence of imprisonment with probation supervision, the execution of the remainder of a sentence that was ordered after a decision on failure to prove oneself during the probationary period in the case of conditional release from execution of a sentence of imprisonment,

e) the convicted person's sentence of home imprisonment has not been converted to a sentence of imprisonment and

f) the convicted person was not in execution of a sentence of imprisonment before committing the criminal offense.

(2) The court shall convert the remainder of the sentence of imprisonment to a sentence of home imprisonment such that one unserved day of the remainder of the sentence of imprisonment equals one day of the sentence of home imprisonment.

Conditional Release from Execution of a Sentence of Imprisonment

## § 66

(1) The court may conditionally release the convicted person if the convicted person has demonstrated improvement in the execution of the sentence by fulfilling his duties and by his conduct and it can be expected that in the future he will lead a proper life, and

a) if it concerns a person convicted of a misdemeanor after serving half of the imposed unconditional sentence of imprisonment or of the unconditional sentence of imprisonment mitigated by a decision of the President of the Slovak Republic,

b) if it concerns a person convicted of a felony after serving two thirds of the imposed unconditional sentence of imprisonment or of the unconditional sentence of imprisonment mitigated by a decision of the President of the Slovak Republic,

c) if it concerns a person convicted of a felony who was not in execution of a sentence of imprisonment before committing the criminal offense after serving half of the imposed unconditional sentence of imprisonment or of the unconditional sentence of imprisonment mitigated by a decision of the President of the Slovak Republic; the court shall simultaneously order control by technical means.

(2) When deciding on conditional release, the court shall also take into account the nature of the criminal offense committed and the type of institution for the execution of sentences in which the convicted person is serving the sentence.

(3) If the convicted person has to serve multiple sentences of imprisonment, the convicted person may be conditionally released at the earliest after serving the sum of the proportionate parts of the imposed sentences under paragraph 1 letters a) to c), § 67 para. 1 and 2 and the entire remainder of the sentence under § 68 para. 2.

(4) In the case of conditional release under paragraph 1 letter c), control by technical means may be terminated at the earliest after the expiration of a period corresponding to two thirds of the imposed unconditional sentence of imprisonment or of the unconditional sentence of imprisonment mitigated by a decision of the President of the Slovak Republic.

## § 67

(1) A person convicted of a particularly serious felony or a person sentenced to imprisonment under § 47 para. 2 except a person sentenced to life imprisonment may be conditionally released only after serving three quarters of the imposed sentence of imprisonment.

(2) A person sentenced to life imprisonment may be conditionally released at the earliest after twenty-five years of execution of this sentence, if, having regard to the circumstances of the act for which he was convicted and the nature of his personality, there is no threat of repetition of the committed act or a similar act through his release.

## § 68

(1) In case of conditional release, the court shall determine a probationary period of one to seven years; the probationary period begins with the conditional release of the convicted person. The court may simultaneously order probation supervision over the convicted person for a period of up to three years and impose appropriate restrictions or obligations referred to in § 51 para. 3 and 4. In case of conditional release from serving a sentence of imprisonment for twenty-five years or for life, the court shall determine a probationary period of ten years and simultaneously order probation supervision over the convicted person for a period of up to five years and impose appropriate restrictions or obligations referred to in § 51 para. 3 and 4. The conditionally released person is obliged to submit to control by technical means if such control is ordered. Control by technical means may be ordered if the conditions according to a special regulation are met. The probationary period does not run during the execution of an unconditional sentence of imprisonment and during the execution of detention.

(2) If the conditionally released person led a proper life during the probationary period and fulfilled the imposed restrictions and obligations, the court shall declare that he/she has proven himself/herself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the remainder of the sentence shall be executed. If the convicted person is serving a sentence of imprisonment, the court shall decide so that the executions of sentences of imprisonment follow one another continuously.

(3) If the court has declared that the conditionally released person has proven himself/herself, it shall be deemed that the sentence was executed on the day he/she was conditionally released.

(4) Likewise, it shall be deemed that the sentence was executed on the day the conditional release occurred if the court, through no fault of the convicted person, has not decided within one year from the expiry of the probationary period that the convicted person shall execute the remainder of the sentence. The court may decide so within two years if criminal prosecution is being conducted against the accused for an intentional criminal offense committed during the probationary period of conditional release.

(5) Repeated conditional release from the execution of the same sentence is not possible.

Conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of house arrest

## § 68a

(1) After serving half of the sentence of house arrest, the court may conditionally waive the execution of its remainder if the convicted person, during the execution of the sentence, by his/her way of life has demonstrated that further execution of this sentence is no longer necessary.

(2) In case of conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of house arrest, the court shall determine a probationary period of up to two years, but not shorter than the remainder of the sentence; the probationary period begins to run on the day following the day of legal force of the decision on conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of house arrest.

(3) The court may impose on a convicted person, in respect of whom the court has conditionally waived the execution of the remainder of the sentence of house arrest, appropriate restrictions or obligations referred to in § 51 para. 3 and 4, aimed at ensuring that he/she leads a proper life. If it is not an obligation to compensate for damage caused by a criminal offense or to pay a debt or overdue maintenance, it shall order probation supervision.

## § 68b

(1) If the convicted person, in respect of whom the remainder of the sentence of house arrest was conditionally waived, led a proper life during the probationary period and fulfilled the imposed restrictions or obligations, the court shall declare that he/she has proven himself/herself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the remainder of the sentence shall be converted into an unconditional sentence of imprisonment so that an unserved day of the sentence of house arrest equals one day of unconditional sentence of imprisonment, and shall simultaneously decide on the manner of execution of this sentence.

(2) If the court has declared that the convicted person has proven himself/herself, it shall be deemed that the sentence was executed on the day the decision on waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of house arrest became final.

(3) Likewise, it shall be deemed that the sentence of house arrest was executed on the day the decision on conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of house arrest became final, if the court, through no fault of the convicted person, has not decided on conversion of the sentence according to paragraph 1 within one year from the expiry of the probationary period, or if criminal prosecution is being conducted against the convicted person for another criminal offense committed during the probationary period, within two years from the expiry of the probationary period.

Conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity

## § 69

(1) After serving half of the sentence of prohibition of activity, the court may conditionally waive the execution of its remainder if the convicted person, during the execution of the sentence, by his/her way of life has demonstrated that further execution of this sentence is no longer necessary.

(2) In case of conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity, the court shall determine a probationary period of up to five years, but not shorter than the remainder of the sentence; the probationary period begins to run on the day following the day of legal force of the decision on this waiver.

(3) The court may impose on a convicted person, in respect of whom the court has conditionally waived the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity, appropriate restrictions and obligations referred to in § 51 para. 3 and 4, aimed at ensuring that he/she leads a proper life; it shall also usually impose on him/her the obligation to compensate, according to his/her capacity, for the damage caused by the criminal offense or a part thereof, which it shall determine simultaneously.

(4) The court may not conditionally waive the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity pursuant to paragraph 1 if it is a sentence of prohibition of the activity of driving a motor vehicle that was imposed on a convicted person,

a) who has already been imposed such a sentence of prohibition of activity in the previous ten years for a criminal offense of endangerment under the influence of an addictive substance or for another criminal offense committed under the influence of an addictive substance, or

b) who has already been penalized in the previous ten years for a similar act committed under the influence of an addictive substance, as referred to in letter a), with a prohibition of the activity of driving a motor vehicle.

(5) The court may not conditionally waive the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity pursuant to paragraph 1 if the sentence of prohibition of activity was imposed pursuant to § 61 para. 3 to 5.

## § 70

(1) If the convicted person, in respect of whom the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity was conditionally waived, led a proper life during the probationary period and fulfilled the imposed restrictions and obligations, the court shall declare that he/she has proven himself/herself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the remainder of the sentence shall be executed.

(2) If the court has declared that the convicted person has proven himself/herself, it shall be deemed that the sentence was executed on the day the decision on waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity became final.

(3) Likewise, it shall be deemed that the sentence of prohibition of activity was executed on the day the decision on conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity became final, if the court, through no fault of the convicted person, has not ordered within one year from the expiry of the probationary period that the convicted person shall execute the remainder of the sentence.

Conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of residence

## § 71

(1) After serving half of the sentence of prohibition of residence, the court may conditionally waive the execution of its remainder if the convicted person, during the execution of the sentence, by his/her way of life has demonstrated that further execution of this sentence is no longer necessary.

(2) In case of conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of residence, the court shall determine a probationary period of up to five years, but not shorter than the remainder of the sentence; the probationary period begins to run on the day following the day of becoming final of the decision on this waiver.

(3) The court may impose on a convicted person, in respect of whom the court has conditionally waived the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of residence, appropriate restrictions and obligations referred to in § 51 para. 3 and 4, aimed at ensuring that he/she leads a proper life; it shall also usually impose on him/her the obligation to compensate, according to his/her capacity, for the damage caused by the criminal offense or a part thereof, which it shall determine simultaneously.

## § 72

(1) If the convicted person, in respect of whom the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of residence was conditionally waived, led a proper life during the probationary period and fulfilled the imposed restrictions and obligations, the court shall declare that he/she has proven himself/herself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the remainder of the sentence shall be executed.

(2) If the court has declared that the conditionally released person has proven himself/herself, it shall be deemed that the sentence was executed on the day the decision on waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of residence became final.

(3) Likewise, it shall be deemed that the sentence of prohibition of residence was executed on the day the decision on conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of residence became final, if the court, through no fault of the convicted person, has not ordered within one year from the expiry of the probationary period that the convicted person shall execute the remainder of the sentence.

## § 72a

Conditional release from the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition on participation in public events

(1) After the execution of half of the sentence of prohibition on participation in public events, the court may conditionally release from the execution of the remainder if the convicted person has demonstrated by his way of life during the execution of the sentence that further execution of this sentence is no longer necessary.

(2) In case of conditional release from the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition on participation in public events, the court shall determine a probationary period of up to five years, but not shorter than the remainder of the sentence; the probationary period begins to run on the day following the day on which the decision on this release becomes final.

(3) For a convicted person in whose case the court has conditionally released from the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition on participation in public events, the court may impose appropriate restrictions and obligations specified in § 51 paragraphs 3 and 4, aimed at ensuring that he leads an orderly life.

## § 72b

(1) If a convicted person in whose case there was a conditional release from the remainder of the sentence of prohibition on participation in public events has led an orderly life during the probationary period and fulfilled the imposed restrictions and obligations, the court shall declare that he has proven himself; otherwise, it shall decide, possibly even during the probationary period, that the remainder of the sentence shall be executed.

(2) If the court has declared that the conditionally released person has proven himself, the sentence shall be deemed to have been executed on the day when the decision on release from the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition on participation in public events became final.

(3) Likewise, the sentence of prohibition on participation in public events shall be deemed to have been executed on the day when the decision on conditional release from the execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition on participation in public events became final, if the court, through no fault of the convicted person, did not order within one year from the expiry of the probationary period that the convicted person shall execute the remainder of the sentence.

Part Five Protective Measures Protective Treatment

## § 73

(1) The court shall impose protective treatment in cases specified in § 39 paragraph 2 letter c) and § 40 paragraph 1 letter c) or if the perpetrator of an otherwise criminal act is not criminally responsible due to insanity and his stay at liberty is dangerous.

(2) The court may do so also if the perpetrator committed a criminal offence

a) in a state of diminished responsibility and his stay at liberty is dangerous,

b) in a state caused by a mental disorder and his stay at liberty is dangerous,

c) of a violent nature against a close person or an entrusted person and with regard to the person of the perpetrator it can be reasonably assumed that he will continue in violent conduct, or

d) under the influence of an addictive substance or in connection with its use.

(3) Protective treatment according to paragraph 2 letter d) shall be imposed by the court on the perpetrator of a misdemeanour under § 171 paragraph 1 or 2 only if the perpetrator consents to its imposition.

(4) Protective treatment shall not be imposed if, with regard to the person of the perpetrator, it is clear that its purpose cannot be achieved.

(5) The court may impose protective treatment also in addition to a sentence or upon waiver of punishment.

## § 74

(1) If protective treatment is imposed in addition to an unconditional sentence of imprisonment, its execution generally begins after commencement of execution of the sentence in a penal institution. In other cases, protective treatment is generally carried out in an institutional health care facility. If, with regard to the nature of the disease and treatment options, it can be expected that outpatient treatment will also fulfill the purpose, the court may also order this method of treatment, or change institutional treatment to outpatient treatment or, in a justified case, vice versa. If the length of execution of the sentence of imprisonment in a penal institution is not sufficient to fulfill the purpose of protective treatment, the court may decide on its continuation in a treatment or outpatient facility.

(2) Protective treatment shall last as long as its purpose requires. However, the duration of protective treatment imposed on a perpetrator who uses an addictive substance and committed a criminal offence under its influence or in connection with its use may be terminated if during its execution it is found that its purpose cannot be achieved. The court decides on release from protective treatment.

(3) The court shall release from the execution of protective treatment if the circumstances for which it was imposed cease to exist before its commencement.

## § 75

Protective Education

The conditions for imposing and executing protective education are regulated in special provisions on prosecution of juveniles.

Protective Supervision

## § 76

(1) The court shall impose protective supervision on a perpetrator whom it sentences for a particularly serious felony to an unconditional sentence of imprisonment.

(2) The court may also impose protective supervision on a perpetrator of an intentional criminal offence who has already been at least twice in the past in execution of a sentence of imprisonment for such a criminal offence and to whom it again imposes an unconditional sentence of imprisonment, if with regard to the person of the perpetrator, particularly taking into account his previous way of life, the environment in which he lives, and the nature of the criminal activity committed, it cannot be expected that after execution of the sentence he will lead an orderly life.

(3) On a motion by the prosecutor or the director of a penal institution, the court may impose protective supervision on a convicted person before the end of execution of a sentence of imprisonment even without fulfilling the conditions specified in paragraph 2, if with regard to the behavior during the previous execution of the sentence of imprisonment it cannot be expected from him that after release from execution of the sentence he will lead an orderly life.

(4) Protective supervision cannot be imposed on a juvenile and a convicted person who has been sentenced to life imprisonment.

## § 77

(1) A convicted person to whom the court has imposed protective supervision is obliged, after release from execution of a sentence of imprisonment, to report within two working days after release from execution of the sentence of imprisonment to the probation and mediation officer of the district court in whose district he has his residence, and within the framework of the imposed protective supervision

a) to report the necessary data on the manner and sources of his livelihood and to prove them,

b) to report in person at specified intervals and

c) to report in advance absence from the place of residence; this does not apply if it concerns regularly recurring absences of which the probation and mediation officer has been informed in advance.

(2) For a convicted person to whom protective supervision has been imposed, the court may also impose additional restrictions or obligations specified in § 51 paragraphs 3 and 4.

(3) A convicted person to whom the court has imposed additional restrictions or obligations according to paragraph 2 is obliged to undergo monitoring by technical means if such monitoring is ordered. Monitoring by technical means may be ordered if the conditions according to a special regulation are fulfilled.

## § 78

(1) Protective supervision is imposed for one year to three years.

(2) If protective supervision is imposed again, before the previously imposed protective supervision has been executed, the time for which it is imposed again, together with the remainder not yet executed of the previously imposed protective supervision, must not exceed five years.

## § 79

Protective supervision ceases

a) upon expiry of the time for which it was imposed, or

b) on the day on which the decision that the conditionally released person has proven himself became final.

## § 80

The court may release from the execution of the remainder of protective supervision if it does not consider its further execution necessary.

Detention

## § 81

(1) If a convicted person during the execution of a custodial sentence is found to have a mental disorder that, according to a professional medical opinion, is incurable and their stay at liberty is, also taking into account the committed criminal activity, dangerous to society, the court, upon a motion by the prosecutor or the director of the penal institution, shall suspend the execution of the custodial sentence and order their placement in a detention facility.

(2) The court may, before the completion of the execution of the custodial sentence, decide on placement in a detention facility also of such a perpetrator of an intentional criminal offense who refuses to undergo protective treatment or in whose case the protective treatment does not fulfill its purpose due to the negative attitude of the patient and whose stay at liberty is dangerous to society; the perpetrator shall be placed in the detention facility after the execution of the custodial sentence.

(3) If the court considers it necessary, it may, before the completion of the execution of the custodial sentence, decide on placement in a detention facility also of a perpetrator of a felony committed from a sexual motive or a perpetrator who repeatedly commits a particularly serious felony; the perpetrator shall be placed in the detention facility after the execution of the custodial sentence.

(4) The court may, on the basis of a professional medical opinion, decide on the placement of the perpetrator in a detention facility also when the perpetrator, whose stay at liberty is dangerous, is undergoing protective treatment in an institutional health care facility and by their conduct endangers the life or health of other persons; the court shall decide upon a motion by the institutional health care facility.

## § 82

(1) The purpose of placement of the perpetrator in a detention facility is to prevent the perpetrator from further commission of criminal offenses and otherwise punishable acts through a special treatment regime and consistent isolation from society.

(2) The stay of the perpetrator in the detention facility lasts until the protection of society from the perpetrator can be ensured by milder means.

(3) At least once a year and always upon a motion by the detention facility, the court shall review the justification for holding the perpetrator in the detention facility and, on the basis of a professional medical opinion, shall decide on the further continuation of detention or on the release of the perpetrator from the detention facility if the reasons for detention have ceased, and shall decide on the further execution of the sentence.

## § 83

Forfeiture of Thing

(1) If the penalty of confiscation of a thing referred to in § 60 paragraph 1 has not been imposed, the court shall impose forfeiture of the thing if

a) it belongs to a person who cannot be prosecuted or convicted,

b) it belongs to a perpetrator from whose punishment the court has refrained, or to a perpetrator against whom criminal prosecution has been discontinued, or to a perpetrator against whom criminal prosecution has been conditionally discontinued, or to a perpetrator against whom criminal prosecution has been discontinued due to approval of a settlement,

c) it does not belong to the perpetrator and is proceeds from criminal activity,

d) it does not belong to the perpetrator and was intended or used for the commission of a criminal offense,

e) it concerns goods without control marks or without other control technical measures required by a generally binding legal regulation for their marking for tax purposes,

f) the circumstances of the case justify the assumption that the thing could be a source of financing of terrorism, or

g) it is required by the safety of people or property, or another similar public interest.

(2) The owner of the forfeited thing becomes the state, unless the court decides otherwise on the basis of a promulgated international treaty by which the Slovak Republic is bound.

(3) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if

a) the injured party has acquired from the act a claim for compensation for damage, the satisfaction of which would be prevented by forfeiture of the thing, or

b) the value of the thing is in manifest disproportion to the degree of seriousness of the misdemeanor.

(4) If the thing referred to in paragraph 1 is unattainable or unidentifiable, or is mixed with the property of the perpetrator or with the property of another person acquired in accordance with the law, the court may impose forfeiture of a thing of such value that corresponds to the value of this thing.

## § 83a

Forfeiture of Part of Property

(1) The court may impose forfeiture of part of property on a perpetrator whom it convicts of an intentional criminal offense for which this law establishes a custodial sentence with an upper limit of the sentencing range of at least four years or for the criminal offense of unauthorized access to a computer system under § 247, unauthorized interference with a computer system under § 247a, unauthorized interference with computer data under § 247b, production and possession of an access device, password to a computer system or other data under § 247d, manipulation in public procurement and public auction under § 266, accepting a bribe under § 328 to 330, bribery under § 332 to 334, indirect corruption under § 336 or distribution of child pornography under § 369, by which the perpetrator obtained or attempted to obtain for themselves or for another a pecuniary benefit and the court is of the opinion that a certain part of their property is proceeds from criminal activity on the basis of proving that the value of the property which the perpetrator acquired or transferred to another person within a period of at most five years before the commission of such a criminal offense, during its commission or after its commission is in gross disproportion to the perpetrator's income acquired in accordance with the law or on the basis of other facts justifying such a conclusion.

(2) The court may impose forfeiture of part of property also when this property could be forfeited under paragraph 1 and if the perpetrator

a) transferred or had transferred this property to another person free of charge or under conspicuously advantageous conditions and this person knew or could and should have known that they transferred such property to them in order to avoid the pecuniary penalty under § 56, the penalty of forfeiture of property under § 58, the penalty of confiscation of a thing under § 60 or the imposition of forfeiture of a thing under § 83, or that this property was acquired in violation of the law,

b) transferred or had transferred to a close person,

c) transferred or had transferred to a legal person in which they themselves or in conjunction with close persons have a majority proprietary participation, a majority share of voting rights or decisive influence on management, and the perpetrator uses such property free of charge or under conspicuously advantageous conditions, or

d) acquired into undivided co-ownership of spouses or this property was located in undivided co-ownership of spouses, which was settled by agreement and belongs to the perpetrator's spouse.

(3) The court is obliged, when imposing forfeiture of part of property, to determine the things that are subject to forfeiture. If a gross disproportion between the value of the property and the perpetrator's income acquired in accordance with the law during the monitored period has been proven, the court may determine any things belonging to the perpetrator in a value up to the amount of the proven gross disproportion.

(4) If it is not known to the court where the things that are the subject of forfeiture of part of property are located, their forfeiture is not appropriate with regard to the rights of third parties, or if the person to whom the thing could have been forfeited destroys, damages, alienates it or otherwise frustrates its forfeiture before the issuance of the decision on forfeiture of part of property, the court may impose forfeiture of a thing of such value or a monetary amount that corresponds to the value of this thing.

(5) Forfeiture of part of property also affects the fruits and benefits of the thing that belong to the person to whom such thing was forfeited. Forfeiture of part of property does not extend to things that are necessary for satisfying the living needs of the person whose part of property is to be forfeited, or of persons whose maintenance and upbringing this person is obliged to provide.

(6) The owner of the forfeited part of property becomes the state, unless the court decides otherwise on the basis of a promulgated international treaty by which the Slovak Republic is bound.

THIRD PART

TERMINATION OF CRIMINAL LIABILITY AND PENALTY

## § 84

Change of Law The criminal liability of an act that had, at the time of commission, the characteristics of a criminal offense specified in the special part of this law, terminates if a subsequent law provides that this act is not a criminal offense.

Effective Remorse

## § 85

Criminal liability for the criminal offenses of spreading a dangerous human infectious disease under § 163, endangering health with defective food and other items under § 168, taking of hostages under § 185, legalization of proceeds from criminal activity under § 233, § 234, breach of duty in the administration of another's property under § 238, violation of regulations on the circulation of goods in relations with foreign countries under § 254, violation of regulations on handling controlled goods and technologies under § 255, § 256, § 257, endangerment of foreign exchange management under § 258, distortion of economic and business records under § 260, damage to the financial interests of the European Union under § 263, endangerment of commercial, banking, postal, telecommunications and tax secrecy under § 264, general endangerment under § 284, damage and endangerment of the operation of public utility facilities under § 286, endangerment and damage to the environment under § 300, § 301, treason under § 311, conspiracy against the Slovak Republic under § 312, terror under § 313, § 314, subversion under § 315, § 316, sabotage under § 317, espionage under § 318, endangerment of classified information under § 319, § 320, endangerment of confidential information and reserved information under § 353, mutiny of prisoners under § 358, spreading of alarming information under § 361, § 362, genocide under § 418, terrorist attack under § 419, certain forms of participation in terrorism under § 419b, financing of terrorism under § 419c, travel for the purpose of terrorism under § 419d, inhumanity under § 425 and use of prohibited means of combat and unlawful conduct of combat under § 426 expires if the perpetrator voluntarily

a) prevented or remedied the harmful consequence of the criminal offense, or

b) made a notification of the criminal offense at a time when the harmful consequence of the criminal offense could still be prevented; the notification must be made to a law enforcement authority or to the Police Force, a soldier may also make this notification to his superior or service authority, and a person serving a sentence of imprisonment or in custody may also make it to a member of the Prison and Judicial Guard Corps.

## § 86

(1) Criminal liability for a criminal offense also expires if it concerns a criminal offense of

a) neglect of mandatory maintenance under § 207, if the criminal offense did not have permanently adverse consequences and the perpetrator subsequently fulfilled his obligation before the court withdrew for final deliberation,

b) non-payment of wages and severance pay under § 214, if the criminal offense did not have permanently adverse consequences and the perpetrator subsequently fulfilled his obligation no later than 60 days from the completion of the criminal offense,

c) abuse of participation in economic competition under § 250, if the perpetrator by his conduct enabled an entrepreneur or other legal entity to fulfill the conditions for non-imposition or reduction of a fine under the law regulating the protection of economic competition,

d) tax and insurance evasion under § 276, non-remittance of tax and insurance under § 277 or non-payment of tax and insurance under § 278, if the perpetrator did not commit the criminal offense as a member of an organized group or as a member of a criminal group and if the due tax and its accessories or insurance were subsequently paid no later than the day following the day when the perpetrator, after the completion of his investigation, could familiarize himself with its results,

e) establishment, organization and support of a criminal group under § 296 or establishment, organization and support of a terrorist group under § 297, if the perpetrator voluntarily made a notification to a law enforcement authority or to the Police Force about the criminal group or terrorist group and its activities at a time when the danger arising from its further activities could still be removed; a soldier may also make this notification to his superior or service authority, and a person serving a sentence of imprisonment or in custody may also make it to a member of the Prison and Judicial Guard Corps,

f) bribery under § 332 or § 333, indirect corruption under § 336 para. 2 and acceptance and provision of undue advantage under § 336d, if the perpetrator provided or promised a bribe or undue advantage only because he was asked to do so, and voluntarily made a notification thereof without delay to a law enforcement authority or to the Police Force; a soldier may also make this notification to his superior or service authority, and a person serving a sentence of imprisonment or in custody may also make it to a member of the Prison and Judicial Guard Corps.

(2) Criminal liability for a criminal offense under paragraph 1 letter d) does not expire if it concerns a perpetrator who was punished for a similar act in the previous twenty-four months, or if criminal prosecution of the perpetrator for a similar act was discontinued in the previous twenty-four months due to the expiration of criminal liability for the criminal offense under paragraph 1.

Statute of Limitations for Criminal Prosecution

## § 87

(1) Criminal liability for an act expires upon the lapse of the limitation period, which is

a) thirty years, if it concerns a felony for which this law permits the imposition of a sentence of life imprisonment,

b) twenty years, if it concerns a felony for which this law in the special part permits the imposition of a sentence of imprisonment with an upper limit of the sentencing range of at least ten years,

c) ten years, if it concerns other felonies,

d) five years, if it concerns a misdemeanor for which this law in the special part permits the imposition of a sentence of imprisonment with an upper limit of the sentencing range of at least three years,

e) three years for other misdemeanors.

(2) The limitation period does not include

a) the period during which it was not possible to bring the perpetrator before court due to a legal obstacle,

b) the period during which the perpetrator stayed abroad with the intention of evading criminal prosecution,

c) the probationary period of conditional discontinuation of criminal prosecution,

d) the period during which the bringing of charges was temporarily postponed, or

e) the period during which criminal prosecution was suspended.

(3) The statute of limitations for criminal prosecution is interrupted by

a) bringing charges for the criminal offense in question, as well as subsequent acts of a law enforcement authority, a judge for preparatory proceedings or a court aimed at criminal prosecution of the perpetrator, or

b) if the perpetrator committed an intentional criminal offense during the limitation period.

(4) Upon interruption of the limitation period, a new limitation period begins.

(5) Criminal prosecution becomes time-barred no earlier than fifteen years after the person against whom the criminal offense of unauthorized removal of organs, tissues and cells and unlawful sterilization under § 159 para. 2, the criminal offense of human trafficking under § 179, the criminal offense of rape under § 199, the criminal offense of sexual violence under § 200, the criminal offense of sexual abuse under § 201 to 202, the criminal offense of abuse of a close person and entrusted person under § 208 and the criminal offense of production of child pornography under § 368 was committed, reached the age of eighteen.

## § 88

Criminal liability for the criminal offenses listed in the twelfth chapter of the special part of this law does not expire upon the lapse of the limitation period, except for the criminal offense of establishment, support and promotion of a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms under § 421, the criminal offense of expression of sympathy for a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms under § 422, the criminal offense of production of extremist material under § 422a, the criminal offense of dissemination of extremist material under § 422b, the criminal offense of possession of extremist material under § 422c, the criminal offense of denial and approval of the holocaust, crimes of political regimes and crimes against humanity under § 422d, the criminal offense of defamation of nation, race and belief under § 423 and the criminal offense of incitement to national, racial and ethnic hatred under § 424.

## § 89

Pardon or mitigation of punishment

(1) The punishment shall not be executed if the President of the Slovak Republic has pardoned it to the convicted person based on their right to pardon punishments. However, this does not apply to other punishments if they were imposed on the offender concurrently and remained unaffected by the decision of the President of the Slovak Republic.

(2) If the President of the Slovak Republic has mitigated the punishment to the convicted person based on their right to mitigate punishments, the punishment as mitigated shall be executed. However, this does not apply to other punishments if they were imposed on the offender concurrently and remained unaffected by the decision of the President of the Slovak Republic.

Limitation of execution of punishment

## § 90

(1) An imposed punishment cannot be executed after the expiration of the limitation period, which is

a) twenty years, if it concerns a conviction to a sentence of life imprisonment,

b) fifteen years, if it concerns a conviction to a sentence of imprisonment exceeding ten years,

c) ten years, if it concerns a conviction to a sentence of imprisonment of at least five years,

d) five years in case of conviction to another punishment.

(2) The limitation period begins with the finality of the judgment and in case of conditional conviction, conditional conviction with supervision, conditional release or conditional release with supervision with the finality of the decision that the punishment shall be executed.

(3) The period during which the punishment could not be executed because the convicted person stayed abroad with the intention to avoid punishment, or a sentence of imprisonment was being executed on them, shall not be counted into the limitation period.

(4) The limitation of execution of punishment is interrupted if

a) the court took a measure aimed at the execution of the punishment which is subject to limitation, or

b) the convicted person committed an intentional criminal offence during the limitation period.

(5) By interruption of the limitation a new limitation period begins.

## § 91

The execution of punishment imposed for criminal offences listed in the twelfth title of the special part of this Act is not subject to limitation, except for the criminal offence of establishment, support and propagation of a movement aimed at suppression of fundamental rights and freedoms pursuant to § 421, the criminal offence of expression of sympathy for a movement aimed at suppression of fundamental rights and freedoms pursuant to § 422, the criminal offence of production of extremist material pursuant to § 422a, the criminal offence of dissemination of extremist material pursuant to § 422b, the criminal offence of possession of extremist material pursuant to § 422c, the criminal offence of denial and approval of holocaust, crimes of political regimes and crimes against humanity pursuant to § 422d, the criminal offence of defamation of nation, race and belief pursuant to § 423 and the criminal offence of incitement to national, racial and ethnic hatred pursuant to § 424.

Expungement of conviction

## § 92

(1) The court may expunge a conviction if the convicted person has led a proper life after the execution or pardon of the punishment, or after limitation of its execution, continuously for a period of at least

a) ten years, if it concerns a conviction to a sentence of imprisonment exceeding five years,

b) five years, if it concerns a conviction to a sentence of imprisonment exceeding one year,

c) three years, if it concerns a conviction to a sentence of imprisonment not exceeding one year.

(2) If it concerns a conviction to punishments listed in § 32 letters b) to l), expungement occurs by their execution.

(3) If the convicted person has demonstrated by their exemplary conduct after the execution or pardon of the punishment or limitation of its execution that they have been rehabilitated, the court may expunge the conviction at the request of the convicted person even before the expiration of the period specified in paragraph 1.

(4) The period specified in paragraph 1, in case where a person conditionally released and conditionally released with supervision is deemed to have served the punishment on the day when the convicted person was conditionally released, is governed by the length of actual execution of the punishment. If the punishment was mitigated by a decision of the President of the Slovak Republic, it is governed by the length of the punishment as mitigated.

(5) A conviction is expunged if ordered by the President of the Slovak Republic based on their right to expunge convictions in the form of pardon or amnesty.

## § 93

(1) If a conviction has been expunged, the offender shall be regarded as if they had not been convicted.

(2) If several punishments were imposed on the offender concurrently, the conviction cannot be expunged until the period for expungement of the sentence of imprisonment pursuant to § 92 para. 1 has expired, if it was imposed.

FOURTH PART

SPECIAL PROVISIONS ON PROSECUTION OF JUVENILES

First Section

Criminal liability

## § 94

General provision

(1) A person who at the time of committing a criminal offence had reached the age of fourteen and had not exceeded the age of eighteen is considered a juvenile.

(2) If this Part does not contain special provisions, other provisions of this Act shall apply to a juvenile.

## § 95

Criminal liability

(1) A juvenile younger than fifteen years who at the time of committing the act had not reached such a level of intellectual and moral maturity to be able to recognize its unlawfulness or control their conduct is not criminally liable for this act.

(2) A misdemeanor, the elements of which are specified in this Act, is not a criminal offence if it was committed by a juvenile and if its seriousness is minor.

Second Section

Termination of criminal liability

## § 96

Limitation of criminal prosecution

(1) The criminal liability of an act terminates by expiration of the limitation period, which is

a) ten years, if it concerns a criminal offence for which this Act establishes a sentence of life imprisonment,

b) five years, if the upper limit of the sentence of imprisonment is at least ten years,

c) three years for other criminal offences.

(2) The criminal liability of those criminal offences where this Act so provides does not terminate by expiration of the limitation period. Third Section

Imposition of sanctions and educational measures on juveniles

## § 97

Purpose of sanctions and educational measures

(1) The purpose of punishment for a juvenile is primarily to educate them to become a proper citizen, while the punishment shall simultaneously serve to prevent unlawful acts and appropriately protect society; the imposed punishment shall simultaneously lead to restoration of disrupted social relationships and to integration of the juvenile into the family and social environment.

(2) The purpose of a protective measure and educational measure for a juvenile is to positively influence the mental, moral and social development of the juvenile with regard to the achieved level of their intellectual and moral development, their personal qualities, family upbringing and environment from which they come, and thereby simultaneously protect the juvenile from harmful influences and society from commission of criminal activity.

(3) When imposing a punishment, protective measure or educational measure, regard must be had to the personality of the juvenile, their age, intellectual and moral maturity, health condition, their personal, family and social circumstances, and they must be proportionate to the nature and seriousness of the committed act and shall lead to integration of the juvenile into the family and social environment so as to prevent unlawful acts.

Waiver of punishment

## § 98

The court may waive punishment of a juvenile who has committed a misdemeanor, regrets its commission and shows effective effort towards rehabilitation, if

a) with regard to the nature of the committed act and the previous life of the juvenile it can be reasonably expected that the hearing of the case before the court alone will suffice for their rehabilitation, or

b) the court accepts a guarantee for the rehabilitation of the juvenile and is of the opinion that, with regard to the educational influence of the person who offered the guarantee, the nature of the committed act and the person of the juvenile, the imposition of punishment does not appear necessary.

## § 99

The court may waive punishment of a juvenile for a misdemeanor also if

a) the juvenile committed the act in a state that was caused by a mental disorder, and the court is of the opinion that protective treatment, which it simultaneously imposes, will ensure the rehabilitation of the juvenile better than punishment, or

b) a protective measure or educational measures are being executed against the juvenile and the imposition of punishment is not necessary to achieve the purpose of the Act.

## § 100

If the court has waived punishment, the juvenile shall be regarded as if they had not been convicted.

## § 101

Conditional waiver of punishment

(1) Under the conditions specified in § 98, the court may conditionally waive punishment of a juvenile if it considers it necessary to monitor the juvenile's behavior during a specified period.

(2) When conditionally waiving punishment, the court shall determine a probationary period of up to one year. At the same time, it may impose on the juvenile appropriate restrictions and obligations specified in § 51 paragraphs 3 and 4, aimed at ensuring that he or she leads a proper life; as a rule, it shall impose on him or her to compensate, to the best of his or her abilities, for the damage caused.

(3) The court may, in view of the circumstances of the case and the person of the juvenile, keep the conditional waiver of punishment in force despite the fact that the juvenile gave cause for imposing a penalty, and

a) appoint probation supervision over the juvenile, if it has not already been imposed,

b) appropriately extend the probationary period, but not by more than one year, or

c) establish educational measures aimed at ensuring that he or she leads a proper life.

(4) If the juvenile from whom punishment was conditionally waived led a proper life during the probationary period and complied with the imposed conditions, the court shall pronounce that he or she has proven himself or herself; otherwise, it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, on imposing a penalty.

(5) If the court has not made a decision pursuant to paragraph 4 within one year from the expiry of the probationary period without the juvenile being at fault for this, he or she shall be deemed to have proven himself or herself.

(6) If it has been pronounced that the juvenile from whom punishment was conditionally waived has proven himself or herself, or if he or she is deemed to have proven himself or herself, he or she shall be regarded as if he or she had not been convicted.

Fourth Part

Protective education

## § 102

Grounds for imposing protective education

(1) If the court convicts a juvenile, it may impose protective education on him or her if

a) the juvenile's education is not properly cared for and this deficiency cannot be remedied in the family in which he or she lives,

b) the juvenile's previous education has been neglected, or

c) the environment in which the juvenile lives does not provide a guarantee of his or her proper education.

(2) Protective education cannot be imposed if the person is a soldier.

## § 103

Execution of protective education

(1) Protective education is carried out in special educational facilities (hereinafter "protective institutional education") or may also be carried out in a professional substitute family (hereinafter "protective family education"); however, if the juvenile's health condition requires it, it is carried out preferentially in an institutional health care facility.

(2) Protective education shall last as long as its purpose requires, but at most until the juvenile reaches the age of eighteen; if the juvenile's interest requires it, the court may extend it until he or she reaches the age of nineteen.

(3) If protective education cannot be carried out immediately, the court shall order supervision by a probation and mediation officer for the period until its commencement.

(4) The court shall waive the execution of protective education if the circumstances for which it was imposed cease to exist before its commencement.

(5) If the re-education of the juvenile has progressed to such an extent that it can be expected that even without the restrictions to which he or she is subject in the educational facility or in the designated family, he or she will behave properly, but all the circumstances for which the protective education was imposed have not yet ceased, the court may conditionally waive the protective education or conditionally place the juvenile outside the educational facility or substitute family. In doing so, it may impose supervision by a probation and mediation officer or other educational measure on the juvenile.

(6) If the juvenile fails to fulfill the expectation that he or she will behave properly without the restrictions to which he or she is subject within the framework of the designated protective education, the court shall revoke the conditional waiver of protective education or conditional placement outside the educational facility or designated family and decide that the execution of protective education shall continue.

Change in the method of execution of protective education

## § 104

(1) Protective institutional education may be changed to protective family education at any time and vice versa.

(2) If it is necessary in view of the purpose of protective education, the natural person to whom the juvenile was entrusted for protective family education may also be changed.

## § 105

If a person who has reached the age of twelve and is younger than fourteen years has committed an act for which this Act permits the imposition of a custodial sentence for life, the court shall impose protective education on him or her in civil proceedings also at the prosecutor's motion; the court may also do so when it is necessary to ensure the proper education of a person younger than fourteen years who has committed an act that would otherwise be a criminal offense or a person younger than fifteen years who has committed the criminal offense of sexual abuse under § 201.

Fifth Part

Educational measures

## § 106

Types of educational measures and their imposition

(1) In case of conditional waiver of punishment or in preparatory proceedings, educational measures may be imposed on the juvenile to achieve the purpose of the Act.

(2) Educational measures are:

- a) educational obligations and restrictions,
- b) admonition with warning.
  - (3) Educational measures may be imposed by the court and, in preparatory proceedings with the consent of the person against whom the proceedings are being conducted, also by the prosecutor. The juvenile may revoke his or her consent at any time during criminal prosecution until the end of preparatory proceedings by a written statement addressed to the prosecutor; the execution of the educational measure thereby ends.
  - (4) In the event that it is proven that complete or timely fulfillment of the educational measure is impossible for the juvenile or that he or she cannot fulfill it for other serious reasons or culpably fails to fulfill it, the court and, in preparatory proceedings, the prosecutor shall revoke or modify the imposed educational measure.

## § 107

Educational obligations and restrictions

(1) The court and, in preparatory proceedings, the prosecutor may impose educational obligations and restrictions on the juvenile, in particular that he or she

a) submit to probation supervision carried out by a probation and mediation officer,

b) reside with a parent or other adult who is responsible for his or her education,

c) endeavor to reach a settlement with the injured party,

d) compensate, to the best of his or her abilities, for the damage caused by the criminal offense or otherwise contribute to removing the consequences of the criminal offense,

e) perform community service free of charge in his or her free time,

f) undergo treatment for harmful addiction,

g) undergo, in his or her free time, a social training program, psychological counseling, psychotherapeutic, educational, additional training, requalification or other suitable program for developing his or her social skills and the personality of the juvenile, which is not a probation program.

(2) The performance of community service of a certain type may be imposed on the juvenile only in such a way that it does not disrupt his or her preparation for a future profession, in particular the fulfillment of obligations related to the school's educational program, or the performance of a profession or employment, and may involve a maximum of four hours per day, eighteen hours per week, sixty hours in total.

## § 108

Admonition with warning

By admonition with warning, the court and, in preparatory proceedings, the prosecutor shall emphatically reproach the juvenile in the presence of his or her legal representative for the unlawfulness of his or her act and warn him or her of the sanctions that he or she faces under this Act if he or she commits criminal activity in the future.

Sixth Part

Punishments

## § 109

Types of punishments

The court may impose on a juvenile only

a) punishment of mandatory work,

b) monetary punishment,

c) punishment of forfeiture of property,

d) punishment of prohibition of activity,

e) punishment of prohibition of participation in public events,

f) punishment of expulsion,

g) punishment of house arrest,

h) punishment of deprivation of liberty.

## § 110

Imposition of punishment

(1) When determining the punishment, the court shall also take into account, as a mitigating circumstance, how the juvenile fulfilled the imposed educational obligations and restrictions specified in § 107.

(2) If the court imposes a cumulative punishment or aggregate punishment on an offender who committed criminal offenses both before he or she exceeded the age of eighteen and after he or she reached that age, it shall proceed in accordance with § 41 and § 42, whereby the limits of sentencing rates for criminal offenses committed before he or she exceeded the age of eighteen are assessed in accordance with § 117.

## § 111

Punishment of Mandatory Work

When imposing a punishment of mandatory work, the upper limit of this punishment must not exceed half of the upper limit of the penalty rate established by law. The punishment of mandatory work must not, due to its nature or the circumstances under which it is carried out, endanger the health, safety or moral development of juveniles.

## § 112

Punishment of Prohibition of Activity

(1) A prohibition of activity may be imposed by the court on a juvenile only if it does not hinder preparation for their profession, and the upper limit of this punishment must not exceed five years.

(2) If the court sentences a juvenile under the conditions of § 61 para. 3, it shall impose a punishment of prohibition of activity in the upper half of the penalty rate specified in paragraph 1.

(3) If the court sentences a juvenile under the conditions of § 61 para. 4, it shall impose a punishment of prohibition of activity for seven to fifteen years.

(4) If the court sentences a juvenile under the conditions of § 61 para. 5, it shall impose a punishment of prohibition of activity for seven to fifteen years.

## § 112a

Punishment of Prohibition from Attending Public Events

The punishment of prohibition from attending public events may be imposed by the court on a juvenile only under the conditions established by this Act, for a period of up to five years.

## § 113

Punishment of Expulsion

Expulsion may be imposed by the court on a juvenile only under the conditions established by this Act, for a period of one to five years. In doing so, it shall also take into account the family and personal circumstances of the juvenile, bearing in mind that the juvenile should not be exposed to the danger of demoralization by this punishment.

## § 114

Monetary Punishment

(1) A monetary punishment in the amount of 30 to 16,590 euros may be imposed by the court upon fulfillment of the conditions established by this Act, if the juvenile is gainfully employed or their property circumstances allow the imposition of this punishment.

(2) If the court imposes a monetary punishment on a juvenile, it shall determine, in the event that its execution could be intentionally frustrated within the prescribed period, a substitute punishment of imprisonment of up to one year. However, the substitute punishment of imprisonment must not, even together with the imposed punishment of imprisonment, exceed the upper limit of the penalty rate reduced according to § 117 para. 1.

(3) After the decision imposing a monetary punishment on a juvenile becomes final, the court may decide, after hearing the juvenile, that its payment or the unexecuted remainder shall be replaced by the convicted juvenile performing community service within a probation program.

## § 115

Conditional Postponement of Execution of Monetary Punishment

(1) The court may conditionally postpone the execution of a monetary punishment if

a) with regard to the person of the juvenile, particularly taking into account their previous life and the environment in which they live and work, and the circumstances of the case, it is of the opinion that the purpose of the punishment will be achieved even without its execution, or

b) it accepts a guarantee for the reformation of the juvenile and if, with regard to the educational influence of the person who offered the guarantee, it is of the opinion that the purpose of the punishment will be achieved even without its execution.

(2) The granting of conditional postponement of a monetary punishment does not affect the execution of other punishments imposed alongside the monetary punishment, unless the court decides otherwise.

## § 116

Probationary Period

(1) In the case of conditional postponement of execution of a monetary punishment, the court shall determine a probationary period of up to three years. At the same time, it may impose on the juvenile appropriate restrictions and obligations specified in § 51 para. 3 and 4, aimed at ensuring that they lead a proper life, and the obligation to submit to monitoring by technical means if such monitoring is ordered; the probationary period begins to run on the day following the day on which the judgment becomes final. Monitoring by technical means may be ordered if the conditions under a special regulation are met.

(2) The period during which the conditionally sentenced person led a proper life during the probationary period and complied with the imposed conditions shall be counted towards the newly determined probationary period in case of conditional sentencing for the same act or towards the probationary period determined when imposing an aggregate punishment or cumulative punishment.

(3) The provisions on conditional sentencing shall apply to decisions on proving oneself during the probationary period.

## § 116a

Punishment of House Arrest

(1) The punishment of house arrest may be imposed by the court on a juvenile upon fulfillment of the conditions established by this Act and with the consent of their legal representative for up to one year.

(2) The court may impose on the juvenile during the execution of the punishment of house arrest educational measures specified in § 107 para. 1, aimed at ensuring that they lead a proper life.

## § 117

Punishment of Imprisonment

(1) The penalty rates of imprisonment established in this Act are reduced by half for juveniles; the upper limit of the reduced penalty rate must not exceed seven years and the lower limit of the reduced penalty rate two years.

(2) An unconditional punishment of imprisonment may be imposed by the court on a juvenile only on the condition that, with regard to the circumstances of the case, the person of the juvenile, or with regard to previously imposed measures, the imposition of another punishment would clearly not lead to achieving the purpose of punishment according to this Act.

(3) In the event that a juvenile has committed a particularly serious felony and the degree of seriousness of such particularly serious felony to society is exceptionally high due to the reprehensible manner of committing the act or due to a reprehensible motive or due to a grave and hardly reparable consequence, the court may impose a punishment of imprisonment of more than seven years up to fifteen years if it is of the opinion that the punishment specified in paragraph 1 is insufficient to achieve the purpose.

(4) The punishment of imprisonment for persons who have not exceeded eighteen years of age is executed in institutions for the execution of punishment for juveniles.

(5) The court may decide that the punishment shall also be executed in an institution for the execution of punishment for juveniles in the case of a juvenile who has already exceeded eighteen years of age; in doing so, it shall take into account in particular the length of the punishment and the degree and nature of the juvenile's disturbance.

## § 118

Extraordinary Reduction of Punishment of Imprisonment

(1) If the court finds that the conditions of § 39 para. 1 or 2 for an extraordinary reduction of the punishment of imprisonment below the lower limit of the penalty rate are met, it is not bound by the limitations under § 39 para. 3 in the case of a juvenile.

(2) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if it concerns a criminal offense for which a punishment of life imprisonment may be imposed according to this Act.

## § 119

Conditional Postponement of Execution of Punishment and Conditional Postponement of Execution of Punishment with Probation Supervision

(1) If the court conditionally postpones the execution of a punishment of imprisonment for a juvenile for a probationary period or conditionally postpones the punishment of imprisonment for them for a probationary period with probation supervision, it shall simultaneously determine a probationary period of one to three years. The juvenile is obliged to submit to monitoring by technical means if such monitoring is ordered. Monitoring by technical means may be ordered if the conditions under a special regulation are met.

(2) The court may, with regard to the circumstances of the case and the person of the juvenile, leave the conditional postponement of execution of punishment or the conditional postponement of execution of punishment with probation supervision in force despite the fact that the juvenile gave cause for ordering the execution of the punishment of imprisonment, and

a) determine probation supervision over the juvenile if it has not already been imposed,

b) appropriately extend the probationary period, but not by more than two years, and it must not exceed the upper limit of the probationary period of five years,

c) impose an additional educational measure specified in § 106 para. 2, aimed at ensuring that they lead a proper life, or

d) order monitoring of imposed educational restrictions or obligations by technical means if the conditions under a special regulation are met and if such monitoring has not been ordered so far.

## § 120

Limitation of Execution of Sentence

(1) An imposed sentence cannot be executed after the expiry of the limitation period, which is

a) ten years, if a sentence of imprisonment was imposed according to § 117 para. 3, or

b) three years for other criminal offences.
