(2) The execution of a sentence imposed for criminal offences listed in the twelfth chapter of the Special Part of this Act is not subject to limitation even in the case of a juvenile, except for the criminal offence of establishment, support and promotion of a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms according to § 421, the criminal offence of expression of sympathy for a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms according to § 422, the criminal offence of production of extremist material according to § 422a, the criminal offence of dissemination of extremist material according to § 422b, the criminal offence of possession of extremist material according to § 422c, the criminal offence of denial and approval of the holocaust, crimes of political regimes and crimes against humanity according to § 422d, the criminal offence of defamation of nation, race and belief according to § 423 and the criminal offence of incitement to national, racial and ethnic hatred according to § 424.

## § 121

Expungement of Conviction

(1) A juvenile who has been imposed or by decision of the President of the Slovak Republic had mitigated or remitted a sentence of imprisonment not exceeding one year shall be considered as if not convicted, from the day of execution of this sentence or from the day when such sentence or its remainder was lawfully waived.

(2) The court shall decide whether the conviction of a juvenile to a sentence of imprisonment, to which paragraph 1 does not apply, is expunged, taking into account the behaviour of the juvenile during the execution of the sentence of imprisonment, after the execution of this sentence. If the sentence was mitigated by decision of the President of the Slovak Republic, the court shall proceed in this manner after the juvenile was released after serving the mitigated sentence.

(3) If the court declared that a juvenile conditionally sentenced to imprisonment, conditionally sentenced to imprisonment with probation supervision or conditionally released from execution of imprisonment has proven himself, he shall be considered as if not convicted.

(4) A juvenile who has been imposed a pecuniary penalty shall be considered as if not convicted, as soon as the sentence has been executed or the execution of the sentence or its remainder has been lawfully waived.

(5) A juvenile who has been imposed a sentence of confiscation of a thing shall be considered as if not convicted, as soon as the sentence has been executed.

(6) A juvenile who has been imposed a sentence of community service or a sentence of prohibition of activity shall be considered as if not convicted, as soon as the sentence has been executed or its execution or its remainder has been lawfully waived.

(7) A juvenile who has been imposed a sentence of expulsion shall be considered as if not convicted, as soon as the period for which it was imposed expires, if the sentence was duly executed.

(8) A juvenile who has been imposed a sentence of prohibition of attendance at public events shall be considered as if not convicted, as soon as the sentence has been executed.

FIFTH PART

INTERPRETATION OF TERMS

First Title

General Terms

## § 122

Criminal Offence and Methods of its Commission

(1) An act also means the omission of such act to which the offender was obliged according to the circumstances and his conditions.

(2) A criminal offence is committed publicly if it is committed

a) through the content of printed matter or dissemination of writings, film, radio, television, use of a computer network or other similarly effective means, or

b) before more than two persons present at the same time.

(3) A criminal offence is committed with a weapon if the offender or, with his knowledge, any of the co-perpetrators uses a weapon for attack, to overcome or prevent resistance or has it on his person for such purpose; a weapon means, unless otherwise provided by a particular provision, any thing by which an attack on the body can be made more forceful. A criminal offence is also committed with a weapon if the offender uses an imitation weapon or has it on his person with the intention that it be considered genuine.

(4) A criminal offence is committed by breaking in if the offender entered an enclosed space by unlawfully overcoming a lock or by overcoming another security obstacle using force or deception.

(5) A criminal offence is committed in a dwelling if it is committed in another's house or apartment or in other premises serving for habitation including premises and land belonging to them, if they are enclosed as part of the dwelling.

(6) A criminal offence is committed by deception if it was committed by exploiting an error which the offender caused, or by using trickery.

(7) A criminal offence is committed by violence if the offender uses physical violence against the bodily integrity of another person to commit it, or if it is committed on a person whom the offender rendered defenceless by deception, or if the offender used violence against another's property.

(8) A criminal offence is committed using duress when the offender uses psychological violence against another to commit it.

(9) A criminal offence is committed repeatedly if the offender successively committed several identical criminal offences by repeated separate acts, between which there is no objective or subjective connection, whereby the punishability of each of them is assessed separately.

(10) A continuing criminal offence is considered to be when the offender continued in committing the same criminal offence. The punishability of all partial attacks is assessed as one criminal offence if all partial attacks by the same offender are connected by an objective connection in time, in the manner of their commission and in the subject of the attack, as well as by a subjective connection, in particular the unifying intention of the offender to commit the said criminal offence.

(11) A collective criminal offence is considered to be its commission by several acts which separately are not yet a criminal offence. The punishability of all such acts is assessed jointly.

(12) A continuous criminal offence is considered to be the causing and maintaining or only maintaining of an unlawful state.

(13) If the accused continues in the conduct for which he is prosecuted, even after notification of the bringing of charges, such conduct from this procedural act is assessed as a new act; this does not apply if it concerns the criminal offence of neglect of obligatory maintenance according to § 207. In such case, it is a continuation of the conduct until the time when the judgment of the court of first instance is pronounced or until the court of second instance retires for final deliberation.

(14) A criminal offence is committed in connection with participation in a public event if it is committed by a participant of a public event at the time and place of the public event or in its vicinity or at another place during movement to the place of the public event or from the place of the public event, including means of transport or service establishments. A public event is considered to be a public assembly, cultural, sporting or other event accessible to the public.

## § 123

Harm to Health

(1) For the purposes of this Act, harm to health means any damage to the health of another.

(2) For the purposes of this Act, bodily harm means such damage to the health of another which objectively required medical examination, treatment or cure during which the usual way of life of the injured person was impaired not only for a short time.

(3) For the purposes of this Act, grievous bodily harm means only a serious health disorder or serious illness, which is

a) mutilation,

b) loss or substantial reduction of working capacity,

c) paralysis of a limb,

d) loss or substantial weakening of the function of a sensory organ,

e) damage to an important organ,

f) disfigurement,

g) causing a miscarriage or killing of a foetus,

h) excruciating suffering, or

i) a health disorder lasting a longer time.

(4) For the purposes of this Act, a health disorder lasting a longer time means a disorder which objectively required treatment, possibly also incapacity for work, for a duration of at least forty-two calendar days, during which it seriously affected the usual way of life of the injured person.

Damage

## § 124

(1) For the purposes of this Act, damage means harm to property or actual loss of property or rights of the injured party or other harm which is causally related to a criminal offense, regardless of whether it is damage to a thing or to rights. For the purposes of this Act, damage also means obtaining a benefit in causal connection with a criminal offense.

(2) Damage within the meaning of paragraph 1 also means loss of profit to which the injured party would otherwise be entitled, given the circumstances and their situation, or which they could reasonably have achieved.

(3) In the case of criminal offenses against the environment, damage means the sum of ecological harm and property damage, whereby property damage also includes the costs of restoring the environment to its previous state. In the case of the criminal offense of unauthorized waste management under § 302, the scope of the act means the price at which waste is usually collected, transported, exported, imported, recovered, disposed of or deposited at the time and place of detection of the act, and the price for removing waste from a place not intended for its disposal.

## § 125

(1) Minor damage means damage exceeding the amount of 266 euros. Greater damage means an amount reaching at least ten times that amount. Substantial damage means an amount reaching at least one hundred times that amount. Damage on a large scale means an amount reaching at least five hundred times that amount. These criteria shall apply equally to determining the amount of benefit, the value of a thing and the scope of the act.

(2) If this Act, in its special part, requires in the basic factual definition the causing of damage as a property consequence of a criminal offense and does not state its amount, it shall be deemed that at least minor damage must be caused.

## § 126

(1) When determining the amount of damage, the price at which the thing that was the subject of the attack is usually sold at the time and place of the act shall be taken as a basis. If the amount of damage cannot be determined in this way, the reasonably incurred costs of acquiring the same or similar thing or of restoring the thing to its previous state shall be taken as a basis.

(2) In the case of harm to the environment, harm caused to protected species of animals and plants, specimens or woody plants, or damage to heritage-protected things or things of historical, artistic or scientific value, when determining the harm or amount of damage, the value of the thing determined by law or other generally binding legal regulation issued on the basis of law shall also be taken as a basis.

(3) If the amount of damage or harm cannot be determined by any of the methods according to paragraphs 1 and 2, or if there are serious doubts about the correctness of the amount of damage or harm thus determined, its amount shall be determined on the basis of an expert opinion or confirmation of a legal entity whose competence or subject of activity provides a guarantee of objectivity in determining the damage or harm; otherwise, the amount of damage shall be determined on the basis of an expert report.

Person

## § 127

(1) A child means a person under eighteen years of age, unless this Act provides otherwise.

(2) A person close to juvenile age means a person who has reached the age of eighteen and has not exceeded the age of twenty-one.

(3) For the purposes of this Act, an elderly person means a person older than sixty years.

(4) For the purposes of this Act, a close person means a relative in direct line, adoptive parent, adoptee, sibling and spouse; other persons in a family or similar relationship shall be considered close persons to each other only if the harm suffered by one of them would rightfully be felt by the other as their own harm.

(5) For the purposes of the criminal offenses of extortion under § 189, rape under § 199 para. 2, sexual violence under § 200 para. 2, sexual abuse under § 201 para. 2, abuse of a close person and entrusted person under § 208, dangerous threatening under § 360 para. 2, dangerous stalking under § 360a, dangerous electronic harassment under § 360b or financing of terrorism under § 419c para. 2, a close person also means a former spouse, partner, former partner, parent of a common child and a person who is a close person to them under paragraph 4, as well as a person who lives or lived with the perpetrator in a common household.

(6) For the purposes of this Act, a sick person means a person who at the time of the act suffers from a physical illness or mental illness, even if temporary, regardless of whether they are temporarily incapacitated for work, as well as a person with altered work capacity, a disabled person or a person with severe health impairment, whereby the intensity of such illness or impairment corresponds to serious bodily harm.

(7) For the purposes of this Act, a defenseless person means a person who, given their age, health condition, circumstances of the act or circumstances on the part of the perpetrator, had no hope of effectively defending themselves against the attack.

(8) For the purposes of this Act, a person entrusted to care or supervision means a person who, given their age or health condition or for another reason, on the basis of a decision of a court or other state authority, on the basis of a contract, is entrusted to another to supervise, care for, provide for or educate them in their household or in a facility designated for this purpose or elsewhere.

(9) For the purposes of this Act, a dependent person means a person who is dependent on the perpetrator for their sustenance, education, material or other provision or care.

(10) An addicted person means a person addicted to addictive substances or to addictive harmful activities.

(11) A subordinate person is a person who, as a result of position, is personally, in employment, in service or otherwise subordinate to the perpetrator by their position, work assignment, function or rank and as a result is obliged to receive and comply with their instructions, orders or commands.

(12) For the purposes of this Act, multiple persons means at least three persons.

## § 128

(1) For the purposes of this Act, a public official means the President of the Slovak Republic, a Member of the National Council of the Slovak Republic, a Member of the European Parliament, a member of the Government, a judge of the Constitutional Court of the Slovak Republic, a judge, a prosecutor or other person holding a position in a public authority, a member of the armed forces, a person in service relationship, a mayor, a chairman of a higher territorial unit, a member of a territorial self-government body, a civil servant or an employee of a state administration body, territorial self-government or other state body, a person who exercises authority within a legal entity entrusted by law with the power to decide in the field of public administration, a notary, a judicial executor, a member of the forest guard, water guard, fishing guard, hunting guard, nature guard or a person who has the authorization of a member of the nature guard, if they participate in the fulfillment of the tasks of society and the state and use the authority that has been entrusted to them within the responsibility for the fulfillment of these tasks. For the criminal liability and protection of a public official, individual provisions of this Act require that the criminal offense be committed in connection with their authority, responsibility, position or function, unless this Act provides otherwise. A public official is also a judge or official of an international judicial body recognized by the Slovak Republic or an official or other responsible employee of a body active in criminal proceedings of another state, a body of the European Union or a body created jointly by member states of the European Union, if they perform acts of criminal proceedings of such state or body on the territory of the Slovak Republic; for their protection under the provisions of this Act, it is required that they perform acts of criminal proceedings in accordance with an international treaty or with the consent of the authorities of the Slovak Republic. In the case of the criminal offense of acceptance and provision of undue advantage under § 336c and § 336d, a public official is also a person who, by virtue of their position or function, has the right to decide or participates in decision-making on the disposal of property, property rights and financial resources, the management of which is subject to control by the Supreme Audit Office of the Slovak Republic.

(2) For the purposes of this Act, a foreign public official means a person

a) holding a position in a legislative body, executive body, judicial body or arbitration body, or in another public administration body of a foreign state, including the head of state,

b) holding a position, employed or working in an international organization or supranational organization created by states or other subjects of public international law in its body or institution, or authorized to act on their behalf,

c) holding a position, employed or working in an international judicial body, or authorized to act on its behalf, or

d) holding a position in a legal entity in which a foreign state has decisive influence, if the exercise of such position is associated with authority in the conduct of public affairs and the criminal offense was committed in connection with this authority or through the use of their position.

(3) For the purposes of this Act, a soldier means

a) a person who performs military service,

b) a member of the armed forces of the sending state for criminal offenses committed on the territory of the Slovak Republic to the extent specified in an international treaty,

c) a member of the Police Force, the Corps of Prison and Judicial Guard, the National Security Authority, the Slovak Information Service and an armed member of the financial administration,

d) a prisoner of war.

(4) Where this Act refers to military service or military duty, this means the service or duty of the persons referred to in paragraph 3(c) only in time of war or state of war.

(5) For the purposes of this Act, a person penalized for a similar act means a person who has been imposed a sanction or other measure for a similar act for a misdemeanor or other similar offense.

(6) For the purposes of this Act, a convicted person means a perpetrator who has been found guilty by a final judgment. This does not apply if the conviction has been expunged.

(7) A punished person means a convicted person who has fully or partially served the sentence imposed by the court. This does not apply if the conviction has been expunged.

(8) If this Act requires a special quality, capacity or status of the perpetrator for the commission of a criminal offense, only a person who has the required quality, capacity or status may be the perpetrator or co-perpetrator of the criminal offense. A person who does not have the required quality, capacity or status may also be a participant in the criminal offense. If the law provides that the perpetrator must be the bearer of a special quality, capacity or status, it is sufficient if this quality, capacity or status is fulfilled by the legal entity on whose behalf the perpetrator acts.

(9) Only a soldier may be the perpetrator or co-perpetrator of a military criminal offense under the eleventh chapter of the special part of this Act.

## § 129

Group of persons and organization

(1) For the purposes of this Act, a group of persons means at least three persons.

(2) For the purposes of this Act, an organized group means an association of at least three persons for the purpose of committing a criminal offense, with a certain division of assigned tasks among individual members of the group, the activity of which is consequently characterized by planning and coordination, which increases the probability of successful commission of the criminal offense.

(3) For the purposes of this Act, an extremist group means an association of at least three persons for the purpose of committing a criminal offense of extremism.

(4) For the purposes of this Act, a criminal group means a structured group of at least three persons that exists for a certain period of time and acts in a coordinated manner with the aim of committing one or more felonies, the criminal offense of legalization of proceeds from criminal activity under § 233 or any of the criminal offenses of corruption under the eighth chapter of the third part of the special part for the purpose of directly or indirectly obtaining financial or other advantage.

(5) For the purposes of this Act, a terrorist group means a group of at least three persons that exists for a certain period of time for the purpose of committing any of the criminal offenses of terrorism, the activity of which is characterized by coordination; it need not have formally defined roles of its members, a permanent membership base or a developed structure.

(6) Activity for a criminal group or terrorist group means intentional participation in such a group or other intentional conduct for the purpose of

a) preserving the existence of such a group, or

b) committing the criminal offenses referred to in paragraphs 4 or 5 by such a group.

(7) Support for a criminal group or terrorist group means intentional conduct consisting in the provision of financial or other resources, services, assistance or in the creation of other conditions for the purpose of

a) establishing or preserving the existence of such a group, or

b) committing the criminal offenses referred to in paragraphs 4 or 5 by such a group. Matter

## § 130

(1) Thing for the purposes of this act means

a) movable thing or immovable thing, apartment or non-residential premises, animal, unless otherwise follows from individual provisions of this act,

b) controllable natural force or energy

c) security regardless of its form.

d) funds in an account,

e) document that is the basis for asserting a legal claim, or

f) property right or other value assessable in money.

(2) A thing is also considered to be intangible information, computer data or image recording on a technical medium.

(3) Entrusted thing for the purposes of this act means a thing owned by another person, which the perpetrator has in authorized use under a contract or for reasons of fulfilling certain tasks according to the disposition of its owner in possession with the obligation to use it only for the agreed purpose or to return it to the owner under agreed conditions.

(4) Appropriation of a thing for the purposes of this act means the removal of a thing from the disposition of the owner or other person who has it lawfully, without consent, with the intent to deal with it as one's own thing.

(5) Addictive substance for the purposes of this act means alcohol, narcotic drugs, psychotropic substances and other substances capable of adversely affecting a person's psyche or their control or recognition abilities, or social behavior.

(6) Means of mass transport for the purposes of this act means a thing capable of transporting at least nine persons.

(7) Extremist material for the purposes of this act means written, graphic, pictorial, audio or audio-visual production

a) of texts and statements, flags, badges, slogans or symbols, of groups and movements that aim or in the past aimed at suppressing fundamental human rights and freedoms,

b) of programs or ideologies of groups and movements that aim or in the past aimed at suppressing fundamental human rights and freedoms,

c) defending, supporting or inciting hatred, violence or unjustifiably different treatment towards a group of persons or an individual because of their belonging to any race, nation, nationality, skin color, ethnic group, origin of family or because of their religious belief, if it is a pretext for the preceding reasons, or

d) justifying, approving, denying or grossly trivializing genocide, crimes against peace, crimes against humanity or war crimes, if the perpetrator or participant of this act was convicted by a final judgment of an international court established under public international law whose jurisdiction was recognized by the Slovak Republic, or by a final judgment of a court of the Slovak Republic.

(8) Material according to paragraph 7 is not considered extremist material if it is demonstrably produced, distributed, put into circulation, made publicly available or stored for the purpose of implementing educational, collection or research activities.

(9) Proceeds of crime for the purposes of this act means

a) a thing that was obtained by a criminal offense,

b) a thing that was obtained as a reward for a criminal offense,

c) a thing that was acquired, even if only in part, for a thing that was obtained by a criminal offense or as a reward for it,

d) a thing into which a thing that was obtained by a criminal offense or as a reward for it was converted, even if only in part, or

e) fruits and benefits of a thing referred to in letters a) to d).

## § 131

(1) Matter of general interest for the purposes of this act means an interest exceeding the scope of individual rights and interests of an individual, which is important from the perspective of the interests of society.

(2) Waste management for the purposes of this act is considered to be collection, transport, export, import, recovery, disposal and storage of waste.

(3) Bribe for the purposes of this act means a thing or other performance of a property or non-property nature to which there is no legal entitlement.

(4) Undue advantage for the purposes of this act means performance of a property or non-property nature capable of influencing a public official, to which there is no legal entitlement and whose value exceeds 200 euros, or unauthorized favoritism of a public official or a person close to them, which is not assessable in money. Undue advantage does not include performance or favoritism, the acceptance of which is, with regard to official or substantively legitimate interest, customary in connection with the position or function of a public official or which is recorded in the published Register of Gifts. Details shall be governed by implementing regulation.

(5) Public document for the purposes of this act means a document issued pursuant to law by a state authority or other public authority within its competence, which establishes, changes or cancels rights or obligations or certifies their creation, change or termination, or certifies the identity of a person or thing, their condition, characteristics or capacity or legally protected interests.

(6) Payment instrument for the purposes of this act means an intangible or tangible protected device, object, record or their combination including a payment card, which is protected against counterfeiting or unauthorized use and which enables the holder or user, alone or together with some procedure or set of procedures, to transfer money, electronic money or virtual currency.

(7) Virtual currency for the purposes of this act means a digital bearer of value that is not issued or guaranteed by a central bank or public authority, is not necessarily linked to legal tender and which does not have the legal status of currency or money, but is accepted by some persons as a means of exchange that can be electronically transferred, stored or electronically traded.

## § 132

Prostitution, pornography and child pornographic performance

(1) Prostitution for the purposes of this act means the satisfaction of sexual needs of another by sexual intercourse, other form of sexual contact or other similar sexual contact with an adult person for remuneration.

(2) Child prostitution for the purposes of this act means the satisfaction of sexual needs of another by sexual intercourse, other form of sexual contact or other similar sexual contact with a child for remuneration or its promise, regardless of whether this remuneration or its promise was provided to the child or a third person.

(3) Pornography for the purposes of this act means depiction of sexual intercourse, other form of sexual contact or other similar sexual contact or depiction of exposed sexual organs intended for sexual purposes.

(4) Child pornography for the purposes of this act means depiction of real or simulated sexual intercourse, other form of sexual contact or other similar sexual contact with a child or a person appearing as a child or depiction of exposed parts of the body of a child or a person appearing as a child intended for sexual purposes.

(5) Child pornographic performance for the purposes of this act means a live performance intended for an audience, including with the use of information technology means, in which a child is involved in real or simulated sexual conduct or in which parts of the child's body are exposed for sexual purposes.

## § 133

Foreign power and foreign agent

(1) Foreign power for the purposes of this act means foreign states and their military or other groupings represented by their organizations and bodies, such as in particular persons performing intelligence activities, military officials, diplomats and other state officials.

(2) Foreign agent for the purposes of this act means a natural person or legal person who, although not an authority or representative of a foreign state, has significant influence in their state or in international relations due to their political, economic or social position.

## § 134

Constitutional Order and Crisis Situation

(1) For the purposes of this Act, constitutional order means a democratic system of fundamental rights and freedoms guaranteed by the proper arrangement and functioning of state authorities, territorial self-government and political parties and movements as regulated by the Constitution of the Slovak Republic.

(2) For the purposes of this Act, crisis situation means

a) state of emergency,

b) state of exception,

c) state of war, or

d) war.

(3) The provisions of paragraph 2(a) and (b) shall not apply if the criminal offence was not committed in connection with a declared state of emergency or state of exception.

## § 135

Possession of Drugs for Personal Use

(1) Possession of a narcotic drug, psychotropic substance, poison or precursor for personal use means to have unlawfully in possession for any period of time a narcotic drug, psychotropic substance, poison or precursor in a quantity corresponding to a maximum of three times the usual single dose for use, for personal consumption.

(2) Possession of a narcotic drug, psychotropic substance, poison or precursor for personal use on a larger scale means to have unlawfully in possession for any period of time a narcotic drug, psychotropic substance, poison or precursor in a quantity corresponding to a maximum of ten times the usual single dose for use, for personal consumption.

## § 135a

Possession of Substances with Anabolic or Other Hormonal Effect

(1) Possession of substances with anabolic or other hormonal effect in a larger quantity means to have unlawfully in possession for any period of time a substance with anabolic or other hormonal effect according to a special regulation in a quantity corresponding to at least ten times one usually indivisible form, in particular one tablet, one injection, one ampoule, or similar indivisible packaging of a preparation containing substances with anabolic or other hormonal effect.

(2) Possession of substances with anabolic or other hormonal effect in a substantial quantity means to have unlawfully in possession for any period of time a substance with anabolic or other hormonal effect according to a special regulation in a quantity corresponding to at least one hundred times one usually indivisible form, in particular one tablet, one injection, one ampoule, or similar indivisible packaging of a preparation containing substances with anabolic or other hormonal effect.

(3) Possession of substances with anabolic or other hormonal effect in a large quantity means to have unlawfully in possession for any period of time a substance with anabolic or other hormonal effect according to a special regulation in a quantity corresponding to at least one thousand times one usually indivisible form, in particular one tablet, one injection, one ampoule, or similar indivisible packaging of a preparation containing substances with anabolic or other hormonal effect.

## § 136

Calculation of Time

Where this Act associates any effect with the expiration of a certain period calculated in days, the day on which the legal fact determining its commencement occurred is not counted.

## § 137

Unauthorised Absence

(1) A person is unauthorised absent who

a) absents themselves without permission from their unit or place of service assignment,

b) fails to report to their unit or place of service assignment, although they were sent there or although the time of their justified absence has expired, in particular upon appointment, transfer, business trip or leave,

c) was separated during combat operations from their unit and failed to rejoin their own or another military unit after the cause of separation ceased, or

d) failed to report to any military unit after return or after release from enemy captivity.

(2) A person is persistently unauthorised absent who absents themselves unauthorised for a period even shorter than forty-eight hours at least three times, if the period between the first and third unauthorised absence does not exceed three months.

(3) Unauthorised absence under paragraph 1 applies only to a person who performs compulsory military service. Second Part

Special Qualifying Terms

## § 138

More Serious Manner of Conduct

A more serious manner of conduct means commission of a criminal offence

a) with a weapon except for the criminal offences of premeditated murder under § 144, murder under § 145, manslaughter under § 147 and § 148, homicide under § 149, bodily harm under § 155, § 156 and § 157,

b) for a longer time,

c) in a cruel or tormenting manner,

d) by violence, threat of imminent violence or threat of other serious harm,

e) by breaking in,

f) by deception,

g) by exploiting distress, inexperience, dependence or subordination,

h) by breach of an important duty arising from the perpetrator's employment, position or function or imposed on them by law,

i) by an organised group, or

j) against several persons.

## § 139

Protected Person

(1) A protected person means

a) a child,

b) a pregnant woman,

c) a close person,

d) a dependent person,

e) a person of advanced age,

f) a sick person,

g) a person enjoying protection under international law,

h) a public official or a person who fulfils their duties imposed by law,

i) a witness, expert, interpreter or translator, or

j) a healthcare worker in the performance of a healthcare profession aimed at saving life or protecting health.

(2) The provision of paragraph 1 shall not apply if the criminal offence was not committed in connection with the position, condition or age of the protected person.

## § 140

Special Motive

A special motive means commission of a criminal offence

a) on order,

b) out of revenge,

c) with the intention to conceal or facilitate another criminal offence,

d) with the intention to commit any of the criminal offences of terrorism,

e) out of hatred towards a group of persons or an individual for their actual or perceived affiliation to any race, nation, nationality, ethnic group, for their actual or perceived origin, colour, sex, sexual orientation, political conviction or religious belief, or

f) with a sexual motive.

## § 140a

Criminal Offences of Extremism

Criminal offences of extremism are the criminal offence of founding, supporting and promoting a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms under § 421, expression of sympathy for a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms under § 422, production of extremist material under § 422a, distribution of extremist material under § 422b, possession of extremist material under § 422c, denial and approval of the holocaust, crimes of political regimes and crimes against humanity under § 422d, defamation of nation, race and belief under § 423, incitement to national, racial and ethnic hatred under § 424, apartheid and discrimination against a group of persons under § 424a and a criminal offence committed from a special motive under § 140(e).

## § 140b

Criminal Offences of Terrorism

Criminal offences of terrorism are the criminal offence of founding, conspiring and supporting a terrorist group under § 297, the criminal offence of terror under § 313 and 314, the criminal offence of terrorist attack under § 419, the criminal offence of certain forms of participation in terrorism under § 419b, the criminal offence of financing terrorism under § 419c, the criminal offence of travel for the purpose of terrorism under § 419d and a felony committed from a special motive under § 140(d).

## § 141

Dangerous Association

A dangerous association means

a) a criminal group, or

b) a terrorist group.

## § 142

Serious Bodily Harm or Death

(1) Serious bodily harm means causing bodily harm referred to in § 123(3).

(2) Death means biological death of the brain (cerebral death).

## § 143

Death of Several Persons

Death of several persons means causing the death of at least three persons.

SECOND PART

SPECIAL PART

FIRST TITLE

CRIMINAL OFFENCES AGAINST LIFE AND HEALTH

First Division

Criminal Offences Against Life

## § 144

Premeditated murder

(1) Whoever intentionally kills another with a premeditated motive shall be punished by imprisonment for twenty to twenty-five years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twenty-five years or life imprisonment if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) and has already been convicted of the criminal offense of murder,

b) on two persons,

c) in a more serious manner of conduct,

d) on a protected person,

e) from a special motive, or

f) with the intention to obtain material benefit.

(3) The offender shall be punished by life imprisonment if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) and has already been convicted for such an act,

b) in a dangerous grouping, or

c) during a crisis situation.

## § 145

Murder

(1) Whoever intentionally kills another shall be punished by imprisonment for fifteen to twenty years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twenty to twenty-five years or life imprisonment if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) on two persons,

b) in a more serious manner of conduct,

c) on a protected person,

d) from a special motive, or

e) with the intention to obtain material benefit.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twenty-five years or life imprisonment if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) and has already been convicted for such an act or for the criminal offense of premeditated murder,

b) in a dangerous grouping, or

c) during a crisis situation.

## § 146

Murder of a newborn child by the mother

A mother who, in a state of distress caused by childbirth, intentionally kills her newborn child during or immediately after childbirth shall be punished by imprisonment for four to eight years.

Manslaughter

## § 147

(1) Whoever, with the intention to cause serious injury to health to another, causes death by negligence shall be punished by imprisonment for seven to ten years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for nine to twelve years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) on a protected person, or

b) from a special motive.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve to fifteen years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of conduct,

b) as a member of a dangerous grouping, or

c) during a crisis situation.

## § 148

(1) Whoever, with the intention to injure the health of another, causes death by negligence shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to ten years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) on a protected person, or

b) from a special motive.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven to twelve years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of conduct,

b) as a member of a dangerous grouping, or

c) during a crisis situation.

## § 149

Causing death

(1) Whoever causes death to another by negligence shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of conduct, or

b) on a protected person.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for four to ten years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 on two or more persons because he grossly violated regulations on occupational safety or traffic or hygiene regulations.

(4) The offender shall be punished in the same way as in paragraph 3 if, in connection with his employment, profession, position or function, or as a driver of a vehicle, he causes death to another by negligence while in a state precluding the ability to perform such activity, which he induced by the influence of an addictive substance.

(5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven to twelve years if, by the act referred to in paragraph 4, he causes death by negligence to two or more persons.

Illegal termination of pregnancy

## § 150

(1) Whoever artificially terminates a pregnant woman's pregnancy without her consent shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for four to ten years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of conduct, or

b) on a protected person.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to fifteen years if, by the act referred to in paragraph 1, he causes serious injury to health or death to the pregnant woman.

## § 151

(1) Whoever, with the consent of a pregnant woman, artificially terminates her pregnancy by a procedure or under conditions that are contrary to generally binding legal regulations on artificial termination of pregnancy shall be punished by imprisonment for two to five years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) and thereby causes serious injury to health or death,

b) on a minor woman without the consent of her legal representative or the person to whom she was entrusted to care or supervision,

c) and thereby obtains greater benefit, or

d) in a more serious manner of conduct.

## § 152

(1) Whoever induces a pregnant woman to

a) artificially terminate her pregnancy herself, or

b) request another or allow another to artificially terminate her pregnancy by a procedure or under conditions that are contrary to generally binding legal regulations on artificial termination of pregnancy, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year.

(2) In the same way as in paragraph 1, whoever assists a pregnant woman in terminating her pregnancy by herself or in requesting another or allowing another to artificially terminate her pregnancy shall be punished.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2

a) in a more serious manner of conduct, or

b) on a protected person.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if, by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2, he causes serious injury to health or death.

## § 153

A pregnant woman who artificially terminates her pregnancy herself or requests another to do so, or allows it, is not criminally liable for such an act, not even under the provisions on instigator and accomplice.

## § 154

Participation in suicide

(1) Whoever incites another to suicide or assists another in suicide, if at least an attempt at suicide occurred, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) he commits in a more serious manner of conduct,

b) on a protected person, or

c) from a special motive.

Part Two

Criminal offenses against health

Injury to health

## § 155

(1) Whoever intentionally causes serious injury to health to another shall be punished by imprisonment for four to ten years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of conduct,

b) on a protected person, or

c) from a special motive.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to fifteen years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) as a member of a dangerous grouping, or

b) during a crisis situation.

## § 156

(1) Whoever intentionally injures the health of another shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to two years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to three years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) on a protected person, or

b) from a special motive.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of conduct,

b) during a crisis situation, or

c) and thereby causes serious injury to health.

## § 157

(1) Whoever causes serious injury to health to another by negligence shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to two years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of conduct, or

b) on a protected person.

## § 158

Whoever injures the health of another by negligence by violating an important duty arising from his employment, profession, position or function or imposed on him by law shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year.

Unauthorized removal of organs, tissues and cells and illegal sterilization

## § 159

(1) Whoever unlawfully removes from a living person an organ, tissue or cell or whoever unlawfully procures for themselves or another such organ, tissue or cell, shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years.

(2) The same punishment as in paragraph 1 shall be imposed on whoever unlawfully sterilizes a natural person.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven to twelve years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1 or 2

a) in a more serious manner of commission,

b) from a special motive, or

c) as a member of a dangerous group.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to fifteen years if by the act specified in paragraph 1 or 2 they cause serious harm to health or death.

## § 160

(1) Whoever unlawfully procures for themselves or another from a dead person an organ, tissue or cell, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of commission, or

b) from a special motive.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1 as a member of a dangerous group.

Third Part

Criminal offences endangering life or health

## § 161

Unauthorized experiment on a human being and cloning of a human being

(1) Whoever under the pretext of obtaining new medical knowledge, methods or to confirm hypotheses, or for clinical testing of medicinal products performs without authorization verification of new medical knowledge

a) despite the fact that human life or health is directly endangered by it, unless it concerns necessary procedures capable of saving their immediately endangered life, or

b) without medical indication and without the consent of the person concerned, or performs it on persons on whom verification without medical indication is prohibited, or performs it on a human fetus or embryo, or performs it in violation of other legal conditions for verification without medical indication, shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if they perform any intervention aimed at creating a human being at any stage of their development genetically identical to another human being, whether living or dead.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1 or 2

a) in a more serious manner of commission,

b) on a protected person, or

c) if by the act specified in paragraph 1 or 2 they obtain for themselves or for another greater benefit.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to fifteen years if by the act specified in paragraph 1 they cause serious harm to health or death.

## § 162

Damage to health

(1) Whoever performs, despite not having the prescribed professional competence of a medical worker or other professional worker in healthcare, acts of medical care and by unprofessional examination or therapeutic procedure or incorrect indication of medicines, medicinal products or other medical devices, even through negligence,

a) directly endangers the life of another,

b) causes harm to the health of another, or

c) carries out without the consent of another an examination or therapeutic procedure or incorrect indication of medicines, medicinal products or other medical devices and by such procedure endangers their health, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of commission, or

b) on a protected person.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if by the act specified in paragraph 1 they cause serious harm to health or death.

Spreading of a dangerous contagious human disease

## § 163

(1) Whoever intentionally causes or increases the danger of introduction or spread of a dangerous contagious human disease, shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1 in a more serious manner of commission.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for four to ten years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and thereby cause serious harm to health or death, or

b) during a crisis situation.

## § 164

(1) Whoever through negligence causes or increases the danger of introduction or spread of a contagious human disease, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of commission, or

b) and thereby cause serious harm to health or death.

Endangering with the human immunodeficiency virus

## § 165

(1) Whoever intentionally exposes another to the danger of infection with the human immunodeficiency virus, shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven to twelve years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of commission, or

b) on a protected person.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to fifteen years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1 and thereby cause serious harm to health or death.

## § 166

(1) Whoever through negligence exposes another to the danger of infection with the human immunodeficiency virus, shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of commission, or

b) on a protected person.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for four to ten years if by the act specified in paragraph 1 they cause serious harm to health or death.

## § 167

Endangering with a sexually transmitted disease

(1) Whoever exposes another, even through negligence, to the danger of sexually transmitted infection, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1 on a protected person.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if by the act specified in paragraph 1 they cause serious harm to health or death.

Endangering health with defective foodstuffs and other items

## § 168

(1) Whoever has for sale, manufactures or intentionally procures for themselves or another defective foodstuffs or other items whose consumption or use for the usual purpose is dangerous to human health, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1 in a more serious manner of commission.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and thereby cause serious harm to health or death, or

b) during a crisis situation.

## § 169

(1) Whoever has for sale or manufactures for such purpose, or through negligence procures for themselves or another foodstuffs or other items whose consumption or use for the usual purpose is dangerous to human health, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1 in a more serious manner of commission.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to five years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and thereby cause serious harm to health or death, or

b) during a crisis situation.

## § 170

Endangering health with unauthorized medicines, medicinal products and medical devices

(1) Whoever, even through negligence, causes or increases the danger of endangering the health of people by

a) unlawfully handling medicines, medicinal products or medical devices, or

b) conducting clinical testing of medicines, medicinal products or medical devices in violation of a generally binding legal regulation, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) when providing medical care,

b) in a more serious manner of commission,

c) on a protected person, or

d) from a special motive.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if by the act specified in paragraph 1 they cause serious harm to health or death.

## § 170a

Unauthorized Handling of Medicinal Products, Pharmaceuticals and Medical Devices

(1) Whoever without authorization on a larger scale

a) manufactures,

b) imports, exports, transports or has transported, or

c) purchases, sells, exchanges, possesses or procures medicinal products or pharmaceuticals that are not authorized under a special regulation, or medical devices that have been placed on the market in violation of a generally binding legal regulation, or whoever intermediates such activity, shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) even though he was convicted for such an act in the preceding twelve months or was punished for a similar act in the preceding twelve months,

b) by a more serious manner of commission,

c) against a protected person,

d) publicly, or

e) on a substantial scale.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and thereby causes grievous bodily harm or death, or

b) on a large scale.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to fifteen years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and thereby causes grievous bodily harm to multiple persons or death of multiple persons, or

b) as a member of a dangerous grouping.

## § 170b

Counterfeiting of Medicinal Products and Medical Devices

(1) Whoever procures for himself or another counterfeit medicinal products or a counterfeit medical device or whoever possesses, imports, exports, transports, offers or sells such medicinal products or medical device shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years.

(2) Imprisonment for one to five years shall be imposed on whoever counterfeits or otherwise alters the identity

a) of a medicinal product including its labelling or states false information about the manufacturer, holder of the decision on registration of the medicinal product or about the country of manufacture with the intention of presenting them as genuine,

b) of a medical device including its labelling or states false information about the manufacturer, about conformity assessment, about the conformity mark, about notification of the medical device or about the country of manufacture with the intention of presenting it as genuine.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 or 2

a) even though he was convicted for such an act in the preceding twelve months or was punished for a similar act in the preceding twelve months,

b) by a more serious manner of commission,

c) against a protected person,

d) publicly, or

e) on a larger scale.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 or 2

a) and thereby causes grievous bodily harm or death, or

b) on a substantial scale.

(5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to fifteen years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 or 2

a) and thereby causes grievous bodily harm to multiple persons or death of multiple persons,

b) as a member of a dangerous grouping, or

c) on a large scale.

Illicit Manufacturing of Narcotic and Psychotropic Substances, Poisons or Precursors, Their Possession and Trading in Them

## § 171

(1) Whoever without authorization possesses a narcotic substance or psychotropic substance from a plant of the cannabis genus for his own use shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year.

(2) Whoever without authorization possesses a narcotic substance or psychotropic substance specified in paragraph 1 for his own use on a larger scale shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years.

(3) Whoever without authorization possesses another narcotic substance or psychotropic substance other than that specified in paragraph 1, poison or precursor for his own use shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years.

(4) Whoever without authorization possesses another narcotic substance or psychotropic substance other than that specified in paragraph 1, poison or precursor for his own use on a larger scale shall be punished by imprisonment for up to five years.

## § 172

(1) Whoever without authorization possesses for any period of time a narcotic substance or psychotropic substance from a plant of the cannabis genus shall be punished by imprisonment for three to seven years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to ten years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 and has already been convicted for such an act.

(3) Whoever without authorization possesses for any period of time another narcotic substance or psychotropic substance other than that specified in paragraph 1, poison or precursor shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years.

(4) Whoever without authorization

a) manufactures,

b) imports, exports, transports or has transported, or

c) purchases, sells, exchanges, procures, a narcotic substance, psychotropic substance, poison or precursor or whoever intermediates such activity shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years.

(5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to fifteen years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 3 or 4 and has already been convicted for such an act.

(6) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to fifteen years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1, 3 or 4

a) for a person who is being treated for drug addiction,

b) by a more serious manner of commission,

c) against a protected person, or

d) on a larger scale.

(7) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for fifteen to twenty years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1, 3 or 4

a) and thereby causes grievous bodily harm or death,

b) towards a person younger than fifteen years or through such a person, or

c) on a substantial scale.

(8) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twenty to twenty-five years or by a sentence of life imprisonment if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1, 3 or 4

a) and thereby causes grievous bodily harm to multiple persons or death of multiple persons,

b) as a member of a dangerous grouping, or

c) on a large scale.

## § 173

(1) Whoever manufactures, procures for himself or another or possesses an object intended for illicit manufacturing of a narcotic substance, psychotropic substance, poison and precursor shall be punished by imprisonment for up to five years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if he gains by the act specified in paragraph 1 for himself or another a larger benefit.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for four to ten years if he gains by the act specified in paragraph 1 for himself or another a substantial benefit.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to fifteen years if he gains by the act specified in paragraph 1 for himself or another a benefit on a large scale.

## § 174

Spreading of Substance Abuse

(1) Whoever induces another to abuse an addictive substance other than alcohol or supports him in that or whoever otherwise incites or spreads the abuse of such a substance shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) towards a protected person, or

b) publicly.

## § 175

Serving of Alcoholic Beverages to Youth

Whoever systematically serves or serves in a larger quantity to a person younger than eighteen years alcoholic beverages shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years.

## § 176

Unauthorized Handling of Substances with Anabolic or Other Hormonal Effect

(1) Whoever unlawfully manufactures, imports, exports, transports, offers, sells, provides to another or administers a substance with anabolic or other hormonal effect according to a special regulation for a purpose other than medical treatment indicated by a physician, or whoever intermediates such activity, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years.

(2) In the same manner as in paragraph 1, whoever with a substance with anabolic or other hormonal effect according to a special regulation

a) unlawfully or without demonstrable medical reason prescribes it on a prescription record or medical prescription or veterinary medical prescription,

b) unlawfully promotes it or unlawfully provides instructions for its use, or

c) unlawfully possesses it in a larger quantity,

shall be punished.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 or paragraph 2

a) although he was convicted in the preceding twelve months for such an act or was sanctioned for a similar act in the preceding twelve months,

b) in connection with organized sports activity,

c) publicly, or

d) in a considerable quantity.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five years to twelve years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 or paragraph 2

a) and causes thereby serious bodily harm or death,

b) by a more serious manner of commission,

c) against a protected person, or

d) in a large quantity.

(5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten years to fifteen years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 or paragraph 2

a) and causes thereby serious bodily harm to multiple persons or death of multiple persons, or

b) as a member of a dangerous grouping.

Failure to Provide Assistance

## § 177

(1) Whoever fails to provide necessary assistance to a person who is in danger of death or shows signs of serious bodily harm, although he can do so without danger to himself or another, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years.

(2) Whoever fails to provide necessary assistance to a person who is in danger of death or shows signs of serious bodily harm, although he is obliged by the nature of his employment to provide such assistance, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years.

## § 178

A driver who, after a traffic accident in which he participated, fails to provide necessary assistance to a person who suffered bodily harm in the accident, although he can do so without danger to himself or another, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years.

SECOND PART

CRIMINAL OFFENSES AGAINST FREEDOM AND HUMAN DIGNITY

First Section

Criminal Offenses Against Freedom

## § 179

Trafficking in Human Beings

(1) Whoever, using fraudulent conduct, deceit, restriction of personal freedom, abduction, violence, threat of violence, threat of other serious harm or other forms of coercion, acceptance or provision of monetary payment or other benefits to obtain the consent of a person on whom another person is dependent, or abuse of his position or abuse of defencelessness or other vulnerable position, recruits, transports, harbours, hands over or receives another, even with his consent, for the purpose of his prostitution or other form of sexual exploitation including pornography, forced labour or forced service including begging, slavery or practices similar to slavery, servitude, forced marriage, abuse for committing criminal activity, removal of organs, tissues or cells or other forms of exploitation, shall be punished by imprisonment for four years to ten years.

(2) In the same manner as in paragraph 1, whoever recruits, transports, harbours, hands over or receives a child, even with his consent, for the purpose of child prostitution or other form of sexual exploitation including child pornography, forced labour or forced service including begging, slavery or practices similar to slavery, servitude, forced marriage, abuse for committing criminal activity, unlawful adoption, removal of organs, tissues or cells or other forms of exploitation, shall be punished.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven years to twelve years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 or 2

a) and obtains thereby for himself or another a greater benefit,

b) and puts another in danger of serious bodily harm or death by such act,

c) as a public official,

d) against a protected person,

e) from a special motive,

f) by a more serious manner of commission, or

g) by joint commission of at least two persons.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve years to twenty years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 or 2

a) and obtains thereby for himself or another a substantial benefit,

b) and causes thereby serious bodily harm or death, or other particularly serious consequence, or

c) as a member of a dangerous grouping.

(5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twenty years to twenty-five years or by a sentence of life imprisonment if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 or 2

a) and obtains thereby for himself or another a benefit of large scale, or

b) and causes thereby serious bodily harm to multiple persons or death of multiple persons.

Entrusting a Child to the Power of Another

## § 180

(1) Whoever, in breach of a generally binding legal regulation, entrusts a child to the power of another for the purpose of adoption, or whoever, in breach of a generally binding legal regulation, obtains a child into his power for the purpose of adoption, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for four years to ten years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and obtains thereby for himself or another a greater benefit, or

b) by a more serious manner of commission.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten years to fifteen years if he causes by the act specified in paragraph 1 serious bodily harm or death, or other particularly serious consequence.

## § 181

(1) Whoever for remuneration entrusts a child to the power of another for the purpose of exploiting him for child labour or for another purpose, shall be punished by imprisonment for four years to ten years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven years to twelve years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and obtains thereby for himself or another a greater benefit, or

b) by a more serious manner of commission.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve years to twenty years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and causes thereby serious bodily harm or death, or other particularly serious consequence, or

b) and obtains thereby for himself or another a substantial benefit.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twenty years to twenty-five years or by a sentence of life imprisonment if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and obtains thereby for himself or another a benefit of large scale,

b) and causes thereby death of multiple persons, or

c) as a member of a dangerous grouping.

## § 182

Deprivation of Personal Freedom

(1) Whoever unlawfully deprives another of personal freedom shall be punished by imprisonment for four years to ten years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven years to twelve years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) from a special motive,

b) against a protected person, or

c) by a more serious manner of commission.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten years to fifteen years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and causes thereby serious bodily harm or death, or

b) and obtains thereby for himself or another a substantial benefit.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for fifteen years to twenty-five years or by a sentence of life imprisonment if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and obtains thereby for himself or another a benefit of large scale,

b) as a member of a dangerous grouping, or

c) and causes thereby death of multiple persons.

## § 183

Restricting Personal Freedom

(1) Whoever without authorization prevents another from enjoying personal freedom shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three years to eight years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of commission,

b) for a special motive,

c) as a public official,

d) on a protected person, or

e) and causes greater damage thereby.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven years to twelve years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and causes serious bodily harm or death thereby, or

b) and causes substantial damage thereby.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve years to twenty-five years or by life imprisonment if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and causes the death of multiple persons thereby,

b) and causes damage of large extent thereby, or

c) as a member of a dangerous grouping.

## § 184

Restricting Freedom of Residence

(1) Whoever by deceit or violence, threat of violence or other harm

a) unlawfully forces another to reside in a certain place, or

b) unlawfully prevents another from residing in a certain place, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of commission,

b) for a special motive, or

c) as a public official.

(3) The same punishment as in paragraph 2 shall be imposed on whoever unlawfully forces another to leave the territory of the Slovak Republic or unlawfully prevents another from residing in the territory of the Slovak Republic.

## § 185

Taking of Hostages

(1) Whoever takes a hostage and threatens to kill him or to cause him bodily harm or other harm with the aim of forcing another to do something, omit something or tolerate something shall be punished by imprisonment for four years to ten years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven years to twelve years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of commission,

b) on a protected person,

c) for a special motive, or

d) and causes greater damage thereby.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten years to fifteen years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and causes substantial damage thereby, or

b) and causes serious bodily harm or death thereby.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for fifteen years to twenty-five years or by life imprisonment if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and causes the death of multiple persons thereby,

b) and causes damage of large extent thereby, or

c) as a member of a dangerous grouping.

## § 186

Kidnapping for Ransom

(1) Whoever takes hold of another against his will, thereby preventing him from enjoying personal freedom, or abducts him and by threat of his death, bodily harm or other harm extorts from him or from a third person the provision of performance of a proprietary or non-proprietary nature shall be punished by imprisonment for seven years to twelve years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten years to fifteen years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and obtains greater benefit for himself or for another thereby,

b) in a more serious manner of commission,

c) on a protected person, or

d) for a special motive.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve years to twenty years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and obtains substantial benefit for himself or for another thereby, or

b) and causes serious bodily harm or death thereby.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twenty years to twenty-five years or by life imprisonment if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and obtains benefit of large extent for himself or for another thereby,

b) and causes the death of multiple persons thereby, or

c) as a member of a dangerous grouping.

## § 187

Abduction Abroad

(1) Whoever abducts another to a foreign country shall be punished by imprisonment for four years to ten years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten years to fifteen years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and causes greater damage thereby,

b) on a protected person,

c) for a special motive, or

d) in a more serious manner of commission.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve years to twenty-five years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and causes serious bodily harm or death thereby, or

b) and causes substantial damage thereby.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for fifteen years to twenty-five years or by life imprisonment if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and causes damage of large extent thereby,

b) and causes the death of multiple persons thereby, or

c) as a member of a dangerous grouping.

## § 188

Robbery

(1) Whoever uses violence or threat of immediate violence against another with the intent to seize another's property shall be punished by imprisonment for three years to eight years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven years to twelve years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and directly endangers the life or health of multiple persons thereby,

b) and causes greater damage thereby,

c) in a more serious manner of commission,

d) on a protected person, or

e) for a special motive.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten years to fifteen years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and causes serious bodily harm or death thereby, or

b) and causes substantial damage thereby.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for fifteen years to twenty-five years or by life imprisonment if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and causes damage of large extent thereby,

b) and causes the death of multiple persons thereby, or

c) as a member of a dangerous grouping.

## § 189

Extortion

(1) Whoever by violence, threat of violence or threat of other serious harm forces another to do something, omit something or tolerate something shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to six years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for four years to ten years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) in a more serious manner of commission,

b) on a protected person,

c) for a special motive,

d) and causes greater damage thereby, or

e) by joint action of at least two persons.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten years to twenty years if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and causes serious bodily harm or death thereby, or

b) and causes substantial damage thereby.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twenty years to twenty-five years or by life imprisonment if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and causes serious bodily harm to multiple persons or the death of multiple persons thereby,

b) and causes damage of large extent thereby, or

c) as a member of a dangerous grouping.

Severe Coercion

## § 190

(1) Whoever by violence, threat of violence or threat of other serious harm forces another to provide performance of a proprietary or non-proprietary nature for himself or for a third person for his own services or services of a third person, which he imposes on him for such performance against his will, even if he pretends such services, shall be punished by imprisonment for four years to ten years.
