(2) In the same way as in paragraph 1, whoever travels from another state to the Slovak Republic for the purpose of committing any of the criminal offenses of terrorism except the criminal offense of terror under § 313 and 314 and the criminal offense of terrorist attack under § 419, shall be punished.

## § 420

Torture and other inhuman or cruel treatment

(1) Whoever in connection with the exercise of authority of a public authority, at its instigation or with its express or tacit consent, causes bodily or mental suffering to another by torment, torture or other inhuman or cruel treatment, shall be punished by imprisonment for two to six years.

(2) By imprisonment for three to ten years the perpetrator shall be punished if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) with at least two persons,

b) by a more serious manner of conduct,

c) on a protected person,

d) from a special motive, or

e) on a person whose personal liberty was restricted in accordance with the law.

(3) By imprisonment for seven to twelve years the perpetrator shall be punished if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and thereby cause serious bodily harm or death,

b) in order to prevent or make more difficult the exercise of another's fundamental rights and freedoms, or

c) as a member of a dangerous grouping.

(4) By imprisonment for twelve to twenty years the perpetrator shall be punished if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and thereby cause serious bodily harm to several persons or death of several persons, or

b) during a crisis situation.

## § 420a

Enforced disappearance

(1) Whoever as a representative of the state or a person or member of a group acting on the basis of authorization, support or tacit consent of the state detains, imprisons, abducts or otherwise deprives another of liberty and subsequently refuses to confirm the deprivation of liberty or conceals the fate or whereabouts of the other and thereby prevents the other from exercising legal protection, shall be punished by imprisonment for seven to twelve years.

(2) In the same way as in paragraph 1, whoever as a superior

a) knew or had information indicating that subordinates subject to their authority committed or intended to commit the act specified in paragraph 1,

b) had factual responsibility for activities and control over activities related to the act specified in paragraph 1, or

c) did not take necessary and appropriate measures within their authority to prevent or suppress the act specified in paragraph 1, or did not report the act specified in paragraph 1 to the relevant authorities for the purposes of investigation or prosecution, shall be punished.

(3) By imprisonment for ten to fifteen years, whoever

a) unlawfully transfers a child on whom the act specified in paragraph 1 was committed, or a child born during the captivity of a mother on whom the act specified in paragraph 1 was committed,

b) unlawfully transfers a child whose father, mother or legal guardian was subjected to the act specified in paragraph 1, or

c) falsifies, conceals or destroys documents confirming the true identity of the child specified in letter a) or b), shall be punished.

(4) By imprisonment for fifteen to twenty years the perpetrator shall be punished if they commit the act specified in paragraphs 1 or 2

a) on a protected person, or

b) thereby cause serious bodily harm or death.

Support and promotion of groups aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms

## § 421

Establishment, support and promotion of a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms

(1) Whoever establishes, supports or promotes a group, movement or ideology that aims to suppress fundamental rights and freedoms of persons, or which preaches racial, ethnic, national or religious hatred, or whoever promotes a group, movement or ideology that in the past aimed to suppress fundamental rights and freedoms of persons, shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years.

(2) By imprisonment for four to eight years the perpetrator shall be punished if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) publicly or in a place accessible to the public,

b) by a more serious manner of conduct, or

c) during a crisis situation.

## § 422

Expression of sympathy for a movement aimed at suppressing fundamental rights and freedoms

(1) Whoever publicly or in a place accessible to the public, particularly by using flags, badges, uniforms or slogans, expresses sympathy for a group, movement or ideology that aims or in the past aimed to suppress fundamental rights and freedoms of persons, or which preaches racial, ethnic, national, religious hatred, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years.

(2) In the same way as in paragraph 1, whoever when committing the act specified in paragraph 1 uses modified flags, badges, uniforms or slogans that create the appearance of genuine ones, shall be punished.

## § 422a

Production of extremist material

(1) Whoever produces extremist material or participates in such production, shall be punished by imprisonment for three to six years.

(2) By imprisonment for four to eight years the perpetrator shall be punished if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) by a more serious manner of conduct, or

b) as a member of an extremist group.

## § 422b

Distribution of extremist material

(1) Whoever reproduces, transports, procures, makes accessible, circulates, imports, exports, offers, sells, sends or distributes extremist material, shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years.

(2) By imprisonment for three to eight years the perpetrator shall be punished if they commit the act specified in paragraph 1

a) by a more serious manner of conduct, | | b) publicly, or

| | c) as a member of an extremist group.

## | § 422c

Possession of extremist material

Whoever possesses extremist material, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years.

## §422d

Denial and approval of the Holocaust, crimes of political regimes and crimes against humanity

(1) Whoever publicly denies, questions, approves or attempts to justify the Holocaust, crimes of a regime based on fascist ideology, crimes of a regime based on communist ideology or crimes of another similar movement that by violence, threat of violence or threat of other serious harm aims to suppress fundamental rights and freedoms of persons, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years.

(2) In the same way as in paragraph 1, whoever publicly denies, approves, questions, grossly trivializes or attempts to justify genocide, crimes against peace, crimes against humanity or war crimes in a manner that may incite violence or hatred against a group of persons or its member, if the perpetrator or participant of this act was convicted by a final judgment of an international court established on the basis of public international law whose jurisdiction was recognized by the Slovak Republic, or by a final judgment of a court of the Slovak Republic, shall be punished.

## § 423

Defamation of Nation, Race and Conviction

(1) Whoever publicly defames

a) any nation, its language, any race or ethnic group, or

b) a group of persons or an individual for their actual or presumed affiliation to any race, nation, nationality, ethnic group, for their actual or presumed origin, skin color, religious belief or for being without belief, shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to three years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) as a member of an extremist group,

b) as a public official, or

c) for a special motive.

## § 424

Incitement to National, Racial and Ethnic Hatred

(1) Whoever publicly incites to violence or hatred against a group of persons or an individual for their actual or presumed affiliation to any race, nation, nationality, ethnic group, for their actual or presumed origin, skin color, sexual orientation, religious belief or for being without belief, or publicly incites to restriction of their rights and freedoms, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years.

(2) In the same manner as in paragraph 1 shall be punished whoever conspires or assembles to commit the act referred to in paragraph 1.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to six years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2

a) for a special motive,

b) as a public official,

c) as a member of an extremist group, or

d) in a crisis situation.

## § 424a

Apartheid and Discrimination of a Group of Persons

(1) Whoever practices apartheid or racial, ethnic, national or religious segregation, or other widespread or systematic discrimination of a group of persons, shall be punished by imprisonment for four years to ten years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight years to fifteen years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) as a member of an extremist group,

b) as a public official,

c) for a special motive,

d) and subjects such group of persons to inhuman or degrading treatment,

e) and puts such group of persons in danger of serious bodily harm or death, or

f) in a crisis situation.

## § 425

Crimes Against Humanity

Whoever, within the framework of a widespread or systematic attack directed against a civilian population, commits

a) murder,

b) extermination of people,

c) enslavement,

d) deportation or forced transfer of population,

e) torture,

f) rape, sexual slavery, forced prostitution, forced pregnancy, unlawful sterilization or other form of sexual violence of comparable gravity,

g) persecution of a group of population on political, racial, national, ethnic, cultural or religious grounds, on the basis of gender or for any other similar reason,

h) imprisonment or other severe deprivation of personal liberty in violation of fundamental rules of international law or any other restriction of personal liberty resulting in the enforced disappearance of persons,

i) apartheid or other similar segregation or discrimination, or

j) other inhuman act, shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve years to twenty-five years or by life imprisonment.

Part Two

War Crimes

## § 426

Use of Prohibited Means of Warfare and Unlawful Conduct of Combat

(1) Whoever during war orders

a) the use of prohibited means of warfare or material of a similar nature, or uses such means or material, or

b) the conduct of combat by prohibited methods, or himself conducts combat in such manner, shall be punished by imprisonment for four years to ten years.

(2) In the same manner as in paragraph 1 shall be punished whoever, as a commander who, in violation of the provisions of international law on means and methods of warfare, intentionally

a) harms by military operation civilian persons in life, health or property, or conducts an attack against them for reasons of reprisals, conducts an attack against an undefended place or demilitarized zone,

b) destroys or damages a dam, nuclear power plant or similar installation containing dangerous forces, or

c) destroys or damages an object dedicated to humanitarian purposes or an internationally recognized cultural or natural monument.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten years to twenty years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 and causes thereby

a) serious bodily harm to several persons or death of several persons,

b) damage on a large scale, or

c) other particularly serious consequence.

## § 427

Pillage in the Area of War Operations

(1) Whoever in the area of war operations, on the battlefield, in places affected by war operations or in occupied territory

a) takes possession of another's property, taking advantage of someone's distress,

b) arbitrarily destroys another's property or takes it under the pretext of war necessity, or

c) robs the fallen or wounded, shall be punished by imprisonment for four years to ten years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven years to twelve years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) by use of violence, threat of violence or other serious harm, or

b) on persons or things enjoying special protection under the law or regulations of international law.

(3) In the same manner as in paragraph 2 the offender shall be punished if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 and causes thereby

a) serious bodily harm, or

b) substantial damage.

(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten years to twenty years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 and causes thereby

a) death, or

b) damage on a large scale.

## § 428

Misuse of Internationally Recognized Designations and State Symbols

(1) Whoever during war misuses the designation of the Red Cross or other distinctive signs or colors recognized by international law for designation of medical institutions, means of transport, persons of medical assistance or evacuation, shall be punished by imprisonment for three years to ten years.

(2) In the same manner as in paragraph 1 shall be punished whoever during war misuses the emblem of the United Nations, the state flag, state emblem, military insignia, badges or uniform of a neutral state or other state which is not a party to the war.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve years to twenty-five years or by life imprisonment if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 and causes thereby as a means of perfidy

a) death of several persons,

b) damage on a large scale, or

c) other particularly serious consequence.

## § 429

Attack Against a Parlementaire

(1) Whoever insults a parlementaire or member of his escort or whoever unlawfully detains such person, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for four years to eight years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 and causes thereby to the parlementaire or member of his escort serious bodily harm.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for seven years to twelve years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 and causes thereby to the parlementaire or member of his escort death.

## § 430

Abuse of the Right of Requisition

(1) Whoever during war

a) abuses the authority entrusted to him in the field of military requisition,

b) carries out requisition although he was not authorized to do so, or

c) refuses to issue a certificate on the type and quantity of items issued in military requisition, shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years.

(2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three years to eight years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1

a) and causes thereby substantial damage,

b) by use of violence, threat of use of violence or threat of other serious harm, or

c) on persons or things enjoying special protection under the law or regulations of international law.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five years to ten years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 and causes thereby damage on a large scale.

## § 431

War Cruelty

(1) Whoever during war violates the provisions of international law by cruelly treating defenseless civilian population, refugees, wounded persons, members of armed forces who have already laid down their arms, or prisoners of war, shall be punished by imprisonment for four to ten years.

(2) The same punishment as in paragraph 1 shall be imposed on whoever during war violates the provisions of international law by

a) failing to implement effective measures for the protection of persons who need such assistance, especially children, women, wounded or elderly persons, or prevents such measures, or

b) preventing or obstructing civil defense organizations of the enemy, neutral or other state in fulfilling their humanitarian tasks.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to twenty-five years or by life imprisonment if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 or 2 and thereby causes serious bodily harm or death or another particularly serious consequence.

## § 432

Persecution of Population

(1) Whoever during war commits inhumane acts arising from national, racial or ethnic discrimination or terrorizes defenseless civilian population by violence or threat of its use, shall be punished by imprisonment for four to ten years.

(2) The same punishment as in paragraph 1 shall be imposed on whoever during the period specified in paragraph 1

a) destroys or seriously disrupts a source of basic life necessities of the civilian population in occupied territory or in a contact zone, or arbitrarily fails to provide the population with assistance necessary for survival,

b) unreasonably delays the return of civilian population or prisoners of war,

c) unreasonably displaces the civilian population of occupied territory,

d) settles occupied territory with the population of its own country, or

e) arbitrarily prevents the civilian population or prisoners of war from having their criminal acts adjudicated in fair proceedings.

(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to twenty-five years or by life imprisonment if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1 or 2 and thereby causes serious bodily harm or death or another particularly serious consequence.

## § 433

War Crimes

(1) Whoever commits an act considered by Article 8 of the Rome Statute of the International Criminal Court to be a war crime shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve to twenty-five years or by life imprisonment.

(2) The offender shall be punished by life imprisonment if he commits the act specified in paragraph 1

a) and thereby causes serious bodily harm to several persons or death of several persons or another particularly serious consequence, or

b) for remuneration.

## § 434

Endangerment of Cultural Property

Whoever during war

a) on a large scale destroys or appropriates objects of cultural value protected by international convention,

b) commits looting, vandalism or other abuse against such object,

c) makes such object a target of attack in violation of international convention, or

d) uses an object of cultural value under enhanced protection of international convention or its immediate surroundings in violation of international convention to support military activity, shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years.

Part Three

Common Provisions

## § 435

(1) For the purposes of applying the provisions of this chapter, war shall be considered

a) an international armed conflict or

b) a protracted armed conflict on the territory of a state between governmental authorities and organized armed groups or between such groups mutually, with the exception of internal disturbances and tensions, such as riots, isolated and sporadic acts of violence or other acts of a similar nature.

(2) A military commander shall also be considered a person factually acting as a military commander.

(3) A military commander is criminally responsible for the criminal offenses specified in this chapter also when they were committed by armed forces under his factual command and control as a result of his failure to control such forces, if

a) he knew or, owing to the circumstances at that time, should have known that the armed forces were committing or were about to commit such criminal offenses, and

b) he did not take all necessary and reasonable measures within his power to prevent or suppress their commission or did not submit the matter to the competent authorities for investigation and prosecution.

(4) A superior, with the exception of the superior specified in paragraph 3, is criminally responsible for the criminal offenses specified in this chapter also when they were committed by his subordinates under his factual authority and control as a result of his failure to properly control such subordinates, if

a) he knew or consciously disregarded information which clearly indicated that the subordinates were committing or were about to commit such criminal offenses,

b) the criminal offenses concerned activities that were within the factual responsibility and control of the superior, and

c) he did not take all necessary and reasonable measures within his power to prevent or suppress their commission, or did not submit the matter to the competent authorities for investigation and prosecution.

PART THREE

COMMON, TRANSITIONAL AND FINAL PROVISIONS

## § 436

Common Provision

This Act transposes legally binding acts of the European Union listed in the annex.

Transitional Provisions

## § 437

(1) If it is necessary to determine whether a criminal offense under Act No. 140/1961 Coll. Criminal Code as amended exhibits the characteristics of a misdemeanor, felony or particularly serious felony under this Act, the provisions of § 10 and § 11 of this Act shall be applied depending on the form of intent and the sentencing rate specified in Act No. 140/1961 Coll. Criminal Code as amended.

(2) When assessing the fulfillment of conditions for deciding on conditional release from serving a prison sentence or deciding on conditional waiver of serving the remainder of the sentence for punishments imposed after the entry into force of this Act for a criminal offense under Act No. 140/1961 Coll. Criminal Code as amended, this Act shall be followed.

(3) When assessing the fulfillment of conditions for deciding on conditional release from serving a prison sentence or deciding on conditional waiver of serving the remainder of the sentence for punishments imposed before the effectiveness of this Act, the existing regulations shall be followed. A person sentenced to an exceptional sentence of imprisonment under § 43 para. 1 of Act No. 140/1961 Coll. Criminal Code as amended may be conditionally released only after serving three-quarters of the imposed prison sentence; a person sentenced under this provision to an exceptional sentence of life imprisonment may not be conditionally released.

(4) If limitation of criminal prosecution or limitation of execution of punishment occurred before the effectiveness of this Act under the existing regulations, the provisions on limitation of criminal prosecution and limitation of execution of punishment under this Act shall not be applied.

(5) The provision of § 47 para. 2 also applies to a person who was punished for any of the criminal offenses specified in § 43 para. 1 of Act No. 140/1961 Coll. Criminal Code as amended, if the person committed another criminal offense specified in § 47 para. 2 after the entry into force of this Act.

(6) The court may decide according to § 34 para. 8 letter d) also when the offender was punished for any of the criminal offenses specified in § 43 para. 1 of Act No. 140/1961 Coll. Criminal Code as amended, which was committed before the entry into force of this Act.

## § 438

(1) If this Act refers to an institution for serving a sentence of minimum, medium or maximum degree of guarding, this shall mean the first, second or third correctional group established under the existing regulations.

(2) If generally binding legal regulations promulgated before the effectiveness of this Act use the term particularly serious criminal offense, this shall mean a felony under this Act.

## § 438a

Transitional Provisions for Amendments Effective from 20 December 2008

The provision of § 269a shall be applied only if the offender or seller was sanctioned by a previous decision after 19 December 2008.

## § 438b

Transitional provisions for amendments effective from 1 January 2009

If the criminality of an act is assessed and a sentence is imposed under the law effective before 1 January 2009, a monetary penalty shall be imposed from 1 January 2009 in such a way that its amount expressed in Slovak crowns shall be converted to euros according to the conversion rate and rounded according to the rounding rules and other rules for the transition to the euro established by a generally binding legal regulation.

## § 438c

Transitional provision for amendments effective from 1 September 2010

The confiscation of a monetary amount under § 83a and the confiscation of property under § 83b may be imposed on a legal person only if the criminal offence referred to in § 83a(1) or § 83b(1) was committed, even in the stage of attempt, or if participation in a criminal offence occurred after 31 August 2010.

## § 438d

Transitional provision for amendments effective from 1 November 2011

The provisions of § 61(3) and (5) shall also apply to a person who was convicted of any of the criminal offences referred to in § 61(3) or (5) before 1 November 2011, if that person committed another criminal offence referred to in § 61(3) or (5) after 1 November 2011. However, the court need not take into account a conviction imposed before 1 November 2011 if, having regard to the exceptional circumstances of the previous cases, the application of the penalty rate for prohibition of activity established in § 61(3) or (5) would be disproportionately harsh for the offender.

## § 438e

Transitional provision for amendments effective from 1 January 2013

A conviction by a court of another Member State of the European Union under § 7b(2) shall not be taken into account if it became final before 1 January 2013.

## § 438f

Transitional provision for amendments effective from 1 August 2013

(1) A penalty of expulsion imposed under Act No. 140/1961 Coll. Criminal Act as amended by later regulations shall be deemed to have been executed after fifteen years from the ordering of its execution; the provision of § 93(2) is not affected by this.

(2) The execution of the penalty of expulsion under subsection 1 shall be notified to the criminal records by the court which decided in the first instance, at the request of the convicted person or even without such a request.

## § 438g

Transitional provision for amendments effective from 1 January 2016

The provisions of this Act on monitoring by technical means effective from 1 January 2016 shall not apply to penalties, proportionate restrictions and obligations, and educational restrictions and obligations that became final before 1 January 2016.

## § 438h

Transitional provision for amendments effective from 1 August 2019

The provisions of § 67(2) and § 68(1) as worded effective from 1 August 2019 shall also apply to a convicted person who was sentenced to life imprisonment under § 34(8) or § 67(3) as worded effective until 31 July 2019.

## § 438i

Transitional provision for amendments effective from 1 January 2021

(1) When assessing whether the conditions for issuing a decision on the imposition of a protective measure of confiscation of part of property under § 83a are met, in determining the amount of gross disproportion between the value of the property that the offender acquired or transferred or had transferred to another person and his income acquired in accordance with the law, only the value of the property that the offender acquired or transferred to another person from 1 January 2021 shall be taken into account.

(2) Where the term "legalizácia príjmov z trestnej činnosti" is used in all its forms in generally binding legal regulations, it shall be understood to mean "legalizácia výnosov z trestnej činnosti" in the appropriate form.

## § 438j

Transitional provision for amendments effective from 1 May 2022

An unconditionally imposed custodial sentence that became final before 1 May 2022 for an act that was assessed as a criminal offence under § 171 or § 172, and which would be assessed as another criminal offence or as a less severely punishable act after 30 April 2022, shall be proportionately reduced by the court after reassessment if the length of the imposed custodial sentence exceeds the upper limit of the penalty rate for such an act after the entry into force of this Act, or if, having regard to the person of the offender and the circumstances of the case, maintaining the original length of the custodial sentence would be disproportionately harsh for the convicted person. In doing so, the court shall take into account the mutual ratio of the penalty rate established for the act before and after the entry into force of this Act. The same principles shall be followed if an aggregate or cumulative sentence was imposed for such an act and a concurrent other criminal offence. The court in whose district the custodial sentence is being executed shall decide on the proportionate reduction of the sentence, even without a motion.

Final provisions

## § 439

The following are repealed:

- 1. Act No. 165/1950 Coll. on the protection of peace,
- 2. Act No. 140/1961 Coll. This Act shall enter into force on 1 January 2006.
